- Key abstractions: Same durable-session-plus-Task-backed-activation model, same `resume?()` capability-as-presence rule, same versioned descriptor.
- Data flow: Same per-activation Task → run → dispose cycle and the same association installed before the provider await (L18, L49).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Preserves the three-lifetime separation and the "provider method presence is the capability" rule (L14, L81).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Same `DURABILITY_FAILED` contract, same ownership-conflict failure, same unmaterialized-child reporting (L35, L51, L53).
- Surprising: Faithfully keeps the message-owned steering receipt and the honest statement that admission is not atomic or exactly-once (L55, L121).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-cross-session-references.md
- Purpose: Lets a user pull relevant work from another conversation into a new message as read-only background, without resuming or forking the source.
- Key abstractions: `@deepseek-ai/dsh-session-reference` at `ctx.sessionReferenceResolver`; the canonical identifier `dsh-session:<base64url(JSON.stringify(sessionId))>`; `ctx.sessionQuery.readSurface(sessionId)`; the compaction checkpoint source marker.
- Data flow: The service's outer `agent/pre-step` listener calls downstream listeners first and processes only an `enter` decision; it parses each accepted direct user message, preserves that message's id while replacing canonical mentions with readable labels, and inserts the frozen snapshot immediately after that message (L30). Preparation deduplicates in first-appearance order, rejects the target id, enforces a limit with a hard maximum of three references, and performs all reads in parallel, returning **no partial context** (L22).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The identifier is JSON-then-base64url, "so quotes, slashes, backslashes, Unicode, newlines, and every other JavaScript string value round-trip without delimiter ambiguity" (L16). The snapshot is serialised as JSON beneath a **fixed untrusted-background warning** telling the model not to follow instructions, permission claims or tool requests from referenced sessions unless the current user repeats them; tag-safe serialisation emits every data `<` as `\u003c`, so source strings cannot spell the surrounding XML-like tags or escape the data region (L26). The snapshot is persisted as a sourced `user/message` immediately after the direct message, so replay satisfies the model-visible/log-reconstructable invariant without a new event type, placement mode or prompt envelope (L26). FTS is **not** a dependency: snapshot correctness needs exact id reads and canonical surface folding, while FTS only improves discovery (L18, L48).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Any read, cancellation, validation or budget error **ends the turn** before the accepted messages enter model-visible history; cancellation races in-flight discovery and exact reads so the listener settles promptly even when a persistence backend cannot interrupt its pending operation (L22). A queued message captures each source when it reaches `agent/pre-step`, so later source messages, compaction, deletion or persistence replacement cannot change the recorded context (L22). Each of at most three references is independently capped at 65,536 UTF-8 bytes; if one source's fixed serialised fields cannot fit its cap, the whole preparation fails rather than emitting a partial context (L44). A resolved non-file target or missing path is a confirmed absence; only a provider exception is temporarily unavailable.
- Surprising: Projection keeps only direct-user messages and steering, completed assistant text, and checkpoint user messages carrying the canonical compaction source — excluding shadowed pre-compaction nodes, tools, reasoning, other plugin user messages and log-only records — so repeated compaction exposes only the latest folded checkpoint lineage still on the current surface (L24). Reference snapshots remain separate sourced user messages, so projection excludes them as injected context and never recursively propagates an earlier snapshot.

- Purpose: Chinese translation of `2026-07-21-cross-session-references.md` — a line-for-line parallel rendering of the cross-session reference decision (same 63 lines, same eight rejected alternatives).
- Key abstractions: Same `ctx.sessionReferenceResolver`, same `dsh-session:<base64url(JSON)>` identifier, same `readSurface` basis.
- Data flow: Same pre-step insert-immediately-after-the-direct-message placement and the same all-or-nothing preparation (L22, L30).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Preserves the JSON-then-base64url round-trip rationale, the fixed untrusted-background warning with `\u003c` escaping, and the no-FTS-dependency rule (L16, L18, L26).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Same per-source 65,536-byte cap with whole-preparation failure, same frozen capture at pre-step (L22, L44).
- Surprising: Keeps the projection exclusion list that prevents recursive snapshot propagation (L24).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-dsh-system-prompt-source-path.md
- Purpose: Tells the agent where its own harness source lives, so a self-referential `cordis` toolset can read the checkout it runs in.
- Key abstractions: the `harness:source` global prompt section registered by `addHarnessSourceSection(ctx, sourceRoot)` in `dsh-app-boot`; the launcher's own module-URL root computation.
- Data flow: The launcher computes the checkout root from `fileURLToPath(new URL('../../..', import.meta.url))` — three hops up from `apps/cli/{src,lib}` — so it resolves however `dsh` is launched; after `boot()` settles the tree it registers a section reading `Your own source code is the checkout at <path>; you can read it there to learn how dsh works and how to extend it.`, ordered at `-99` (just after the harness identity opener at `-100`, before the deployment persona at `0`) (L14).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The testable logic lives in `dsh-app-boot`, not `apps/cli`, because `apps/*` are not coverage-gated while `packages/*` are — the launcher keeps only the thin glue, covered by the CLI's PTY e2e (L16). The path is **not** a config field: it is mechanically the launcher's own location, and a config field invites a stale hand-entered path and a knob with no legitimate variation (L32). `process.cwd()` and `process.argv[1]` are both wrong — the cwd is the user's project and a PATH symlink makes `argv[1]` the symlink path, so `import.meta.url` is the only handle on the real source location (L34).
- Edge cases / failure handling: When the booted tree has no `systemPrompt` service the helper is a no-op returning `undefined` (L16). The section is registered against the booted service's own fiber, so a dev HMR reload of the system-prompt plugin drops it until the next boot — a dev-only wrinkle, since production HMR watches the config, not the built lib (L24).
- Surprising: The line sits **before per-request content**, so it does not perturb the KV cache across turns (L38) — a prompt-construction detail with a caching consequence. Only the `dsh` CLI adds the section; the demo bins boot their committed trees verbatim and gain none, because they are not the self-modification surface (L20).

- Purpose: Chinese translation of `2026-07-21-dsh-system-prompt-source-path.md` — a line-for-line parallel rendering of the self-source-path decision (same 38 lines, same four rejected alternatives).
- Key abstractions: Same `harness:source` section at order `-99`, same `dsh-app-boot` helper.
- Data flow: Same module-URL root computation and same registration after `boot()` settles (L14).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Preserves the coverage-gate argument for putting the logic in a package and the "path is not config" rule (L16, L32).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Same no-op when no `systemPrompt` service, same dev-HMR drop (L16, L24).
- Surprising: Keeps the KV-cache note: the section sits before per-request content so it does not perturb the cache across turns (L38).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-follow-instruction-symlinks.md
- Purpose: Reverses an earlier no-follow rule so a symlinked instruction file loads its target, moving the trust boundary out of the prompt plugin.
- Key abstractions: the agent-instructions candidate probe (`nodeStatFile` vs `fsStatFile`); the per-directory content dedup; the `ctx.fs` confinement boundary.
- Data flow: Discovery no longer inspects the final component with `lstat`; every candidate — the user-global `$DSH_HOME/AGENTS.md`, each base candidate and each local-overlay candidate — is resolved and its **resolved target** stat-ed, at baseline composition and at each `tools/post-execute` reconciliation alike (L14).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The decision is an explicit, **owner-accepted trade-off**, not an oversight: following repository-owned links crosses the plugin's trust boundary because a cloned untrusted repository can carry an `AGENTS.md` whose symlink target is any file the process can read (L20). The mitigation is the filesystem layer, not this plugin — confine `ctx.fs` with the `fs-observation-policy` gate or an OS sandbox when a deployment loads untrusted repositories. Following only the user-global candidate and keeping no-follow for project files was rejected as a split rule that is harder to reason about than one consistently applied policy (L26). Rejecting targets outside the project root was rejected because it reintroduces a partial trust boundary in the wrong layer — path geometry rather than read authority (L28).
- Edge cases / failure handling: A symlink to a regular file loads that target; a resolved **non-file** target (including a link to a directory) is a confirmed absence that removes the scope like a missing file; a `resolve` or `stat` exception is classified as *temporarily unavailable* and never removes an already-loaded scope (L14). The plugin no longer depends on `ctx.fs.lstat` for instruction loading.
- Surprising: The change also fixes an awkward special case: a `CLAUDE.md` that symlinks its sibling `AGENTS.md` now resolves to identical content and collapses through ordinary per-directory content dedup instead of being skipped as a special case (L10, L16).

- Purpose: Chinese translation of `2026-07-21-follow-instruction-symlinks.md` — a line-for-line parallel rendering of the symlink-following decision (same 32 lines, same three rejected alternatives).
- Key abstractions: Same resolve-then-stat probe in place of `lstat`, same dedup participation.
- Data flow: Same resolved-target stat at baseline and at each reconciliation (L14).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Preserves the owner-accepted trust trade and the "the filesystem layer is the mitigation" argument (L20).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Same non-file-target-as-absence vs exception-as-temporarily-unavailable split (L14).
- Surprising: Keeps the observation that the change turns the `CLAUDE.md → AGENTS.md` mirror from a special case into an ordinary duplicate (L10, L16).
### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-instruction-load-all-dedup.md
- Purpose: Loads *every* instruction candidate in a directory and deduplicates only when two files are effectively the same, replacing the one-winner-per-directory rule.
- Key abstractions: per-candidate scope keys encoded `directory\u0000candidateName`; `candidateScopeKey` / `decodeScopeKey`; `probeScopeInstruction`; the version cache's `trimmedDigest`.
- Data flow: Every existing candidate in each list loads — base list first, then local list — in configured order. Within one directory, candidates whose content is byte-identical **after trimming leading and trailing whitespace** collapse to the earliest candidate, and the kept file's original bytes are rendered (L14). Dedup is enforced during reconciliation, not only at baseline: each pass rebuilds a per-directory set of kept trimmed-content digests in candidate order, so an unchanged file is removed when an earlier candidate converges on its content (L24).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Because a scope now names **one fixed file**, the previous "candidate switch within a scope" — an `AGENTS.md` scope falling through to `CLAUDE.md` and recording `previousPath` — can no longer occur, so `previousPath` was removed from the change record, the serialised `context/message` metadata and the render text; a change is now `set`, a same-file `replace` or `remove` (L22). Trimming before comparison tolerates a trailing newline or an indentation reflow while the survivor still renders its original bytes (L14, L32). Global cross-directory dedup was rejected: identical boilerplate under two directories is legitimately in scope for each, and the deeper file must still surface for work under the deeper directory (L30).
- Edge cases / failure handling: The scope key uses a NUL separator that cannot occur in a real path. Removing one candidate emits a `remove` for that candidate's own scope, leaving a distinct sibling as an independent scope. The `trimmedDigest` lets the fast path re-evaluate duplication without re-reading content.
- Surprising: The note records that first-wins was making the survivor depend on **candidate order rather than on whether the files actually differ** — "exactly the surprise the request set out to remove" (L10, L28). The visible behaviour difference is confined to transition repositories carrying two distinct real files.

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-instruction-load-all-dedup.zh.md
- Purpose: Chinese translation of `2026-07-21-instruction-load-all-dedup.md` — a line-for-line parallel rendering of the load-all-and-dedup decision (same 38 lines, same four rejected alternatives).
- Key abstractions: Same NUL-separated per-candidate scope keys, same `trimmedDigest` cache field.
- Data flow: Same base-then-local loading with trimmed-content collapse to the earliest candidate, enforced during reconciliation (L14, L24).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Preserves the removal of `previousPath` once a scope names one fixed file, and the "order rather than difference" critique of first-wins (L22, L28).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Same per-candidate `remove` scope semantics and same NUL-separated key (L22).
- Surprising: Keeps the note that cross-directory dedup was rejected because deeper instructions must still surface (L30).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-local-instruction-overlay.md
- Purpose: Makes the git-ignored `.local.` convention additive and on by default, instead of a shadowing opt-in.
- Key abstractions: the second candidate list `localInstructionFileCandidates`, defaulting to `['AGENTS.local.md','CLAUDE.local.md']`, resolved with the same same-directory validation as the base list; the per-candidate scope key.
- Data flow: In every project directory from the root to the session cwd the plugin loads the base candidates and then, **additively**, the local candidates; the local files are ordered after the base files so their guidance takes precedence within the byte budget (L14). Both lists load in full under per-directory content dedup.
- Non-obvious design decisions: The default lives in the plugin `Config` schema rather than a product `cordis.yml`, so every embedder (TUI, ACP, headless) reads `.local.` files consistently and a deployment overrides or disables it in one place (L16). The fixed user-global `$DSH_HOME/AGENTS.md` has no local overlay and stays base-only. Higher-priority first-wins was rejected because a personal overlay that *replaces* the committed file drops shared project guidance whenever the overlay exists — the opposite of the additive model the names evoke (L26).
- Edge cases / failure handling: An empty `localInstructionFileCandidates` disables the overlay. The base and local candidates in one directory stay independent across baseline freezing, the pending window, the version cache and reconciliation, so a change to one never suppresses the other — which is why the bare directory could not be reused as the scope key (L22, L32).
- Surprising: The note explains that the packages guidance to keep opt-ins out of shipped defaults is **outweighed** here by strong prior art and the user-facing expectation that `.local.` files are always read (L28). Extending the overlay to the user-global scope is deferred because `$DSH_HOME` is a single fixed `AGENTS.md` with no committed base to supplement (L34).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-local-instruction-overlay.zh.md
- Purpose: Chinese translation of `2026-07-21-local-instruction-overlay.md` — a line-for-line parallel rendering of the default local overlay decision (same 38 lines, same five rejected alternatives).
- Key abstractions: Same second candidate list and its plugin-schema default.
- Data flow: Same additive base-then-local ordering per project directory (L14).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Preserves the plugin-schema (not product-config) default and the "overlay must not replace shared guidance" rule (L16, L26).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Same empty-list disable and same independent per-candidate scopes (L22).
- Surprising: Keeps the explicit override of the "no opt-ins in shipped defaults" packages guidance and the deferred user-global overlay (L28, L34).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-log-backed-session-titles.md
- Purpose: Gives a session a durable, human-facing title — first a deterministic fallback, then an optional model summary — stored on the event log.
- Key abstractions: `ctx.sessionTitle` (`@deepseek-ai/dsh-session-title`) with a registry for **at most one** asynchronous provider; `dsh-session-title-llm`; the log-only `session/title` and `session/title-llm-request` events; `foldSessionTitle()` returning a `SessionTitleSnapshot`; `ctx.sessionQuery.readTitle()`.
- Data flow: Every accepted revision is a log-only `session/title` event whose payload holds normalised non-empty text, the exact eligible human `user/message` seqs used to derive it, and either the fallback source kind or the provider id plus optional route (L20). Before an auxiliary title-model dispatch the shared helper appends a log-only `session/title-llm-request` event with the provider id, source seqs, route, system prompt, messages and output-token cap, so a later generation failure leaves the request auditable. The dispatched envelope is deep-frozen to match the record exactly but carries no process-local agent-loop request identity, so loop-only reconstruction checks do not compare it with the main conversation header.
- Non-obvious design decisions: Titles are **not** session metadata: a mutable `SessionHeader` or side metadata was rejected because it creates a second persistence mutation protocol, weakens immutable identity metadata, makes crash atomicity backend-specific and gives forks ambiguous copy-versus-reference behaviour (L52). Putting titles in derived history or the request prefix was rejected because UI metadata would consume tokens, change cache identity and make the main model observe its own label (L54). Only **one** provider may be registered — a second live registration throws immediately — because completion order is not product precedence and would make retries, HMR and the recorded provider nondeterministic (L55). A user-sourced title **pins** the session: `onUserMessage` schedules no automatic revision while it stands; an explicit `refresh()` is the deliberate unpin (L42).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Each session-local generation has a monotonic revision and exact registration identity, and acceptance rechecks revision, registration, session liveness, service liveness and cancellation, so stale output cannot commit (L34). Concurrent refreshes reserve their revision *before* provider work, so a newer call aborts and supersedes an older one before either can invert completion order; automatic work and refreshes share one session-local in-flight fallback promise so the first fallback creates only one title event (L28). Oversized auxiliary input is **rejected rather than truncated**, because truncation would make the recorded source seqs falsely imply complete use (L56). The fused deadline is checked while consuming each stream chunk and after completion, so a successful result returned after timeout cannot be accepted even when an interceptor ignores abort (L36). Automatic provider failures are nonfatal warnings and retain the latest title; explicit refresh failures reject to the caller.
- Surprising: The `GenerateOptions` carries `purpose: 'session-title'`, and the DeepSeek adapter maps that purpose to **thinking-disabled** and omits reasoning effort so the bounded output is visible title text, while the main conversation keeps its configured thinking mode (L36). Auxiliary request records and late accepted titles consume event seqs without consuming turn numbers, so model history and KV-cache identity do not change (L65). The TUI sets the terminal window title to `<session title> — <configured product title>` after terminal-safe rendering.

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-log-backed-session-titles.zh.md
- Purpose: Chinese translation of `2026-07-21-log-backed-session-titles.md` — a line-for-line parallel rendering of the log-backed title decision (same 67 lines, same seven rejected alternatives).
- Key abstractions: Same `ctx.sessionTitle` service, same one-provider registry, same `session/title` / `session/title-llm-request` events.
- Data flow: Same snapshot payload and same pre-dispatch request record (L20).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Preserves the "titles are not session metadata" reasoning, the single-provider rule and the user-pin semantics (L42, L52, L55).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Same revision/registration recheck before commit, same no-truncation rule, same per-chunk deadline check (L28, L36, L56).
- Surprising: Faithfully keeps the `purpose: 'session-title'` thinking-disabled mapping and the note that title events consume seqs but not turn numbers, leaving model history and KV-cache identity untouched (L36, L65).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-tui-auto-pane-title.md
- Purpose: Gives each TUI session a distinct terminal pane title, so one agent per tmux pane is distinguishable at a glance.
- Key abstractions: `TuiConfig.autoTitle` (default `false` here); the OSC 0 `runtime.terminal.setTitle` path; the `titleSettled` one-shot latch.
- Data flow: When on, the TUI issues one background model call after the first user message of a fresh session, using a fixed task instruction (summarise the request as a two-to-five lowercase-word title, no punctuation) plus the user's first message and **no tools**; it takes the first non-empty line of the reply and caps it at 40 characters (39 plus an ellipsis) (L19). The call reaches `ctx.llm.stream` directly rather than through `agent.send`, so it never appends to the session or transcript and cannot perturb the agent loop (L22).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The label is a **model summary**, not a truncation of the prompt — a truncated raw prompt is noisy, often begins with boilerplate and rarely reads as a title (L27). OSC 0 sets only `pane_title`, so it labels the pane without renaming or leaking into the user's window title (L29). The call is fire-and-forget: awaiting the title before sending the user's message would add latency to the actual request (L35).
- Edge cases / failure handling: The `titleSettled` latch pre-settles when the feature is off and on a resumed session whose first `user/message` is already logged; a whitespace-only first message is skipped without consuming the slot; any failure, empty reply, missing `llm` service or missing agent provider/model leaves the static title untouched; a dedicated `AbortController` cancels an in-flight request on shutdown (L21).
- Surprising: Because the title call stamps `sessionId`, it **shares the session's `llm-replay` cursor**, so enabling `autoTitle` in a replay-backed snapshot would consume a recorded script entry — which is why the default is off and the scripted PTY fixture answers with a tool-branching adapter instead of replay (L40). The feature was later superseded by the log-backed session-title service.

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-tui-auto-pane-title.zh.md
- Purpose: Chinese translation of `2026-07-21-tui-auto-pane-title.md` — a line-for-line parallel rendering of the auto-pane-title decision (same 42 lines, same two supersession banners).
- Key abstractions: Same `autoTitle` config, same OSC 0 path, same one-shot latch.
- Data flow: Same single tool-less model call after the first user message, with the 40-character cap (L19, L22).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Preserves the model-summary-not-truncation rule and the OSC 0 pane-only rationale (L27, L29).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Same latch pre-settle cases and same static-title fallbacks (L21).
- Surprising: Keeps the shared-`llm-replay`-cursor consequence that forced the default off (L40).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-tui-auto-title-default-on.md
- Purpose: Flips `autoTitle` on by default and makes a resumed session re-derive its title, because an off-by-default feature ships inert.
- Key abstractions: `autoTitle` defaulting to `true` in both the zod schema and `resolveTuiConfig`'s `?? true`; the resume scan over `agent.session.events` for the first `user/message`.
- Data flow: On mount, `createTuiChat` scans the session's events for the first logged `user/message` and feeds its text to the same one-shot `generateTitle`; the title is never persisted (the session header carries no title field), so it is always derived, never restored (L17). The latch is now simply `titleSettled = !resolved.autoTitle`, so on resume the title runs once from the stored first message and then latches, and a message arriving *after* the resume does not re-title (L18).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Default-on is **inert where the call cannot run** — a deployment without an `llm` service or a provider/model keeps the static title — so the flip is safe (L16). The keyless-replay concern that motivated off-by-default is addressed by **pinning `autoTitle: false` in the replay-backed snapshot scenarios** rather than by suppressing the feature for every deployment (L23, L32). Persisting the derived title in the session header was rejected: the header has no title field, and adding one would make a terminal label into session metadata — the boundary the auto-title note already drew against the log-backed session-title work (L25). Re-deriving from the *latest* message was rejected because the title summarises what the session is *about*, which its opening request captures (L27).
- Edge cases / failure handling: The replay-backed `examples/tui-agent/tests/tui.snapshot.ts` must opt out because `installLlmReplay` fails loud on an unrecorded request; the unit snapshot mounts no `llm` service so the default flip is inert there (L32). The former "resumed session never fires" test was rewritten to assert re-derivation from the stored first message and that a later live message does not re-title.
- Surprising: The note's stated principle is blunt: "off-by-default ships an inert feature; the descriptive name is only useful if it is the normal experience" (L23). This decision was later superseded by the session-title consolidation, which removed TUI-local title generation entirely.

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-tui-auto-title-default-on.zh.md
- Purpose: Chinese translation of `2026-07-21-tui-auto-title-default-on.md` — a line-for-line parallel rendering of the default-on auto-title decision (same 33 lines, same supersession banner).
- Key abstractions: Same `autoTitle` default flip and the same resume re-derivation scan.
- Data flow: Same mount-time scan of the first logged `user/message` feeding the one-shot generator (L17, L18).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Preserves the "inert where the call cannot run" argument, the snapshot-level opt-out, and the rejection of persisting a title in the session header (L16, L23, L25).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Same replay opt-out requirement and same rewritten resume test (L32).
- Surprising: Faithfully keeps the blunt "off-by-default ships an inert feature" principle (L23).
### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-tui-banner-sweep.md
- Purpose: Replaces the slow per-character slogan typewriter with a whole-banner left-to-right sweep, and deletes the slogan bank outright.
- Key abstractions: `HeaderComponent`'s `revealWidth` clip; pi-tui's `truncateToWidth` (the same ANSI-aware clipper the header already uses); `stopBannerReveal`.
- Data flow: The banner wipes in left-to-right over ~24 frames at 15 ms (~360 ms total, ~60 fps), started after `ui.start()` succeeds and cleared through the same `detachListeners` path the typewriter used; `stopBannerReveal` also resets the clip so a header disposed mid-sweep re-renders whole (L17).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The user's judgement was that the **quotes themselves**, not just their content, were wrong for the surface — so merely changing the copy was rejected (L12, L22). A per-line top-down animation was rejected because with only four lines it would have four visible steps, "closer to a flicker than a reveal"; the horizontal sweep uses the full terminal width for smooth motion at the same total duration (L24). Character-level clipping reuses `truncateToWidth` so the sweep cannot tear escape sequences (L26).
- Edge cases / failure handling: A configured `welcome` renders the whole banner immediately with no animation, keeping fixtures and snapshots frame-deterministic. The PTY smoke's boot marker changes from the typewriter cursor `▌` to the banner's top-right corner `╮`, which only renders once the sweep completes (L18).
- Surprising: Boot output is animation-dependent again but **no longer random** — every boot sweeps the same banner, and the `STARTUP_SLOGANS`/`pickStartupSlogan` exports are gone with no consumer outside the deleted tests (L30, L31). This note was itself superseded: the banner was removed entirely, taking the sweep with it.

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-tui-banner-brand-gradient.md
- Purpose: Paints the banner's `DEEPSEEK` wordmark with the deepseek.com brand gradient, as a single documented exception to the theme-agnostic palette.
- Key abstractions: the per-letter 24-bit truecolor foreground; the three gradient stops `#4D6BFE` → `#3982FF` → `#2498FF`; the `truecolor` config field; `gradientText` / `brandColorAt` helpers; the `themeViolations()` snapshot gate.
- Data flow: The banner paints `DEEPSEEK` with a per-letter truecolor foreground sweeping the brand gradient via piecewise-linear interpolation across the three stops, while `HARNESS` stays bold with the default foreground; the gradient is **foreground-only**, so it stays legible on any terminal background, and it is confined to the banner's product name (L16). The gradient is gated on `resolved.color && resolved.truecolor`; when truecolor is unavailable the banner falls back to the existing flat bright-blue accent (L18).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The TUI palette is deliberately theme-agnostic (standard 16-colour ANSI only, so a user's scheme remaps every colour) and a `themeViolations()` gate rejects any RGB, extended-palette or explicit-background cell — so the brand gradient is the *sole sanctioned exception*, and the fence is mechanically enforced: the `banner-gradient` checkpoint asserts the violations are non-empty and that every violation ends in `rgb-fg`, while every other checkpoint keeps the strict `.toEqual([])` assertion (L12, L26). The gradient stops are treated like a **protocol constant**, hardcoded in the plugin rather than exposed as a tunable, because they are brand identity; whether truecolor is *enabled* is terminal- and deployment-varying, so that is the validated `Config` field (L22).
- Edge cases / failure handling: `truecolor` has no schema default; when unset, `apply()` auto-detects it at the process boundary from `COLORTERM` (`truecolor` or `24bit`), and an explicit config value always wins. Detection reads `process.env` **only** in `apply()` — never in the pure `resolveTuiConfig` resolver — keeping the resolver a pure function of its input and the TUI testable with a fake terminal (L20, L36). The banner text is UI-only and never reaches a model request, so no session event is required.
- Surprising: The note records the cost honestly: "one narrow, documented crack in the theme-agnostic invariant — a fixed-color surface that will not adapt to a user's terminal scheme, accepted because it is brand identity and foreground-only" (L40). A theme-safe stepped gradient from the 16-colour palette was rejected by the requester because 16 fixed colours cannot reproduce the smooth logo gradient, and recolouring the whole palette was narrowed to the banner only (L30, L32).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-tui-borderless-banner.md
- Purpose: Brings the banner back **without the box**, after the no-banner variant was judged wrong.
- Key abstractions: the borderless `HeaderComponent`; `truncateToWidth`; the `stopBannerReveal` lifecycle; `welcome` as a banner subtitle.
- Data flow: Each line is a single leading space plus `truncateToWidth`-clipped content, so the sweep's width clip can never tear an escape sequence and no fixed frame is drawn; the reveal advances through ~24 frames at 15 ms each (L14). The header carries the title (`DEEPSEEK HARNESS`), a `<model>  •  <session-id>` detail line, and — when `welcome` is set — a muted subtitle; with `welcome` unset it is title + detail only, with no fixed or random slogan (L15).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The user verdict was "just remove the border" — the four-row box frame was the objectionable chrome, not the identifying facts (model, session id) nor the sweep-in motion (L10). The model **also** stays in the footer's left segment, so the driving model remains glanceable after the transient banner scrolls away — "the banner is transient and scrolls away with the transcript, while the footer keeps the model visible for the whole session; that persistent location is deliberately preserved" (L16, L32). `welcome` reverts to a banner subtitle because putting it in the transcript made presentation configuration behave like conversation content (L17, L28). The sweep animates **only** when `welcome` is unset, so a configured welcome keeps fixtures frame-deterministic (L18).
- Edge cases / failure handling: `stopBannerReveal` also resets the clip so a header disposed mid-sweep re-renders whole. `/clear` empties the transcript but **not** the header, so the banner and its configured subtitle survive `/clear` — unlike a transcript-based welcome line (L41).
- Surprising: The note owns the current result of three discarded startup variants (random slogans with a typewriter, a boxed whole-banner sweep, and no banner), and the model now appears twice at boot — banner detail and footer — as intentional redundancy (L20, L40). Anything that anchored on banner absence re-anchors on its presence, so the PTY smoke boots on the detail line's `main-session-` id revealed late in the sweep (L43).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-tui-footer-cache-hit-rate.md
- Purpose: Adds a provider-cache reuse signal to the TUI footer, so the user can see whether the reused request prefix is paying off.
- Key abstractions: `TokenTotals` (four disjoint buckets: `input`, `output`, `cacheRead`, `cacheWrite`); `addUsage`; `cacheHitRate(totals)`; `sessionTokens`.
- Data flow: `cacheHitRate` is `round(cacheRead / (input + cacheRead + cacheWrite) * 100)` and `undefined` before any input is billed; `FooterComponent` omits the whole `  cache N%` segment while the rate is `undefined` (L17). Totals are rebuilt on mount by `sessionTokens`, which sums usage over `assistant/message` events — **never** `assistant/chunk`, to avoid double counting — and updated live from each `assistant/message` event that carries usage (L19).
- Non-obvious design decisions: `↑` keeps meaning **uncached** input, not billed input: the disjoint-bucket convention holds across the footer, and the cache percent supplies the reuse signal the raw counts cannot (L18). Showing billed input as `↑` was rejected because it would redefine `↑` away from the disjoint `inputTokens` bucket the rest of the harness reports (L23). Computing the rate against all tokens was rejected because output tokens are never cache-served, so folding them into the denominator understates the rate for no meaning (L25). Dropping `cacheWrite` from the denominator was rejected because cache writes are billed input the provider spent to populate the cache, so excluding them overstates the hit rate on a writing turn (L27).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Rendering `cache 0%` on an empty session was rejected — the ratio is `0/0` and "a `0%` badge on a fresh session is a lie about a value that does not exist yet"; the segment stays hidden until input is billed (L29). A missing `cacheReadTokens`/`cacheWriteTokens` is treated as zero.
- Surprising: The metric got a place in the layout only after the alternatives were priced: giving it its own right-aligned element was rejected because it derives from the adjacent token counts and reads best in the `input → output → cache` order, and grouping it left keeps the lower-priority `tools:` indicator as the element that clips first under width pressure (L31, L35).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-tui-no-banner.md
- Purpose: Removes the boxed startup banner entirely, moving the model into the footer and the welcome into the transcript.
- Key abstractions: `HeaderComponent` (deleted); the footer's left segment (`<model>  <cwd>  ↑tokens ↓tokens`); `rebuildTranscript`'s welcome notice.
- Data flow: The TUI mounts straight into the transcript; `welcome`, when configured, renders as the transcript's first line (a muted notice) inside `rebuildTranscript` so palette swaps preserve it; the PTY smoke's boot marker becomes the footer's model name, the only mounted-TUI text guaranteed to render regardless of cwd length (L17).
- Non-obvious design decisions: A one-line header was rejected because the only load-bearing fact was the model name and the footer already aggregates session status — "a dedicated header row for one fact is the same chrome, smaller" (L24). The session id was **not** moved into the footer because a 36-char UUID dominates the 100-column footer and clips the status segment; it identifies the session for resume, which is a log/filesystem concern, not a glanceable one (L25). Printing the welcome outside the transcript was rejected because "any fixed region above the transcript is a banner again"; as a transcript line it scrolls away naturally and survives rebuilds through the same path as every other transcript element (L27).
- Edge cases / failure handling: `/clear` now wipes the welcome line too, because it is an ordinary transcript line and `/clear` empties the transcript — the old banner survived `/clear` only by sitting outside it (L35). The footer's left segment is wider, so on narrow terminals the right status segment clips earlier.
- Surprising: The note is another reversal in the banner series (slogans → sweep → no banner → borderless banner) and is explicit that it supersedes the sweep note entirely; the model's persistent footer home it added survives into the final design (L8, L9).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-tui-reload-command.md
- Purpose: Adds an experimental, dev-only `/reload` command so a config edit the HMR watcher missed can still be applied without restarting the TUI.
- Key abstractions: `ctx.loader.entries()` and each file-backed subtree's `refresh()` — the exact code path the HMR watcher's config-change branch drives; the include `patches` mechanism.
- Data flow: `/reload` walks `ctx.loader.entries()` and calls `refresh()` on every file-backed `Include`, invoked manually and watcher-independent. Unchanged files are no-ops (content comparison in `Include.read`); invalid files warn and keep the running tree (the hot-reload-resilience contract); include `patches` — including the dsh CLI's personal overlay — re-apply on every re-read (L14).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The TUI reaches the Loader **structurally** (`ctx.loader` via a local type, not `inject`), so tests and embedders running without a Loader degrade `/reload` to a warning notice instead of failing the mount; declaring `loader` in `inject` would make it a hard dependency and break every Loader-less composition (L16, L22). Widening the HMR watch roots to `packages/`/`apps/` was rejected: plugin-source changes reload every dependent plugin's fiber, and the repo's dense shared packages make that a teardown of the spine and the UI mid-session — "a restart in disguise with partial-reload hazards" (L10, L20). `/reload` refreshes **configs only**; module-source hot reload stays watcher-owned.
- Edge cases / failure handling: A re-entrancy guard serialises reloads — `/reload` while one is in flight is refused with a warning, keeping the loader's unmutexed tree-update pass single-writer, and the guard releases on completion or failure (L30). `/reload` runs only while the agent is idle, because a reload can dispose and re-mount entries and could tear tools out from under in-flight calls; the check is advisory but removes the common footgun (L31). If `refresh()`'s never-reject contract ever changes, the command reports the failure instead of leaving an unhandled rejection (L32). Per-file failures surface only in loader logs, which the TUI does not display — acceptable for a dev-only surface and noted in the completion message (L29).
- Surprising: The feature exists because editors that replace files by rename (BSD `sed -i`, `git checkout`) produce **no in-place `change` event**, so the watcher never fires at all (L10). A `cordis_reload` model-facing tool was rejected: this is an operator action for the human at the terminal, not a capability the model should trigger (L24).
### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-tui-resume-command.md
- Purpose: Turns `/resume` from a printed shell command into a real keyboard-driven picker that safely hands the terminal to the resumed session.
- Key abstractions: the TUI's full-viewport interactive overlay seam; `session-query.readSession()` (a detached complete log validated by the same core replay boundary as resume); `TuiRuntime.handoffResume`; the launcher-owned resume identity.
- Data flow: The picker orders candidates by last logged activity and searches log-backed title or id; each row shows current/live/persisted state, last turn outcome, recent provider/model, durable goal phase when present, and the id as secondary text; the current session and sessions already live in this runtime stay visible but disabled (L14). Selecting a candidate revalidates the log, workspace, route, the current agent's idle status and those exclusions, so a stale listing cannot bypass preflight. After preflight the TUI flushes the current session, confirms the agent is still idle, then **stops the terminal** before calling `TuiRuntime.handoffResume`; the shipped `dsh` host disposes the root app and uses `process.execve` with a normalised `--resume` argument, atomically replacing the process rather than starting a child (L18).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The TUI **never executes shell text** — the host seam receives only a validated `SessionId`, because the old text was display copy, not trusted argv (L20, L24). Constructing the resumed agent inside the existing TUI was rejected: replacing one config-created agent would cross Loader ownership, scoped plugin setup, persistence retirement and terminal lifecycle in the presentation layer; root disposal plus process replacement reuses the supported startup path (L26). A missing adapter is **not** a missing session: storage validity and current route availability are independent facts, so the row is kept and the unavailable provider/model is named (L28).
- Edge cases / failure handling: A candidate load failure is local to that row. Persisting goal activation across resume was rejected: durable intent is not authorisation to continue after a human or process boundary — the goal phase survives, automatic continuation does not, and the TUI asks for confirmation or `/goal resume` (L18, L30). Concurrent processes can select or resume the same persisted session because preflight does not serialise them (L34). Without a host, the command fallback stays usable and the session is reported as still resumable.
- Surprising: The search editor "starts immediately after the search glyph and emits pi-tui's cursor marker, so terminal IME composition remains anchored in the field", and Escape clears a non-empty query before a second Escape closes the picker (L14). Process replacement intentionally restarts Loader composition: runtime-only state is rebuilt while only logged or header-backed session state survives (L36).
### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-tui-skill-slash-command.md
- Purpose: Lets a human, not just the model, pull a skill body into a turn — the `/skill:<name> [instructions]` command.
- Key abstractions: `renderSkillInvocation(skill, instructions)`; the shared independent model-and-user invocation policy (`isUserInvocable`); `ctx.get('skills')` as an optional read.
- Data flow: On submit the command loads the named skill and delivers one text block as a user turn — `agent.send()` while idle and `agent.steer()` while running, the same rule as ordinary editor input. The block is a `<skill name="…">` element wrapping the skill body, preceded by one resource-base line when the provider exposes one, with the user's trailing text appended after a blank line (L14).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The TUI reads the skill service through `ctx.get('skills')` rather than a declared injection, because skills mount conditionally — a deployment without the registry keeps a working front door and `/skill:` there reports that skills are unavailable rather than failing to mount (L16). `createTuiChat` is synchronous while `ctx.skills.list()` is async, so autocomplete seeds the static slash commands immediately and rebuilds the provider with `skill:<name>` entries once the catalog resolves; a resolution arriving after disposal is dropped and a rejected lookup keeps the base commands (L16). The TUI has its **own** renderer, deliberately not reused from the tool result's `<skill_content>` shape: a manual invocation is a *user turn* and a tool result is a *tool result*, so sharing one renderer would force a single model-facing shape to serve both surfaces (L20).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Autocomplete filters the invocation-neutral `list()` result with `isUserInvocable`, and manual submission applies the same predicate after a trusted `get()` resolves the definition — so a user-only skill can load even when model invocation is disabled, and a user-disabled skill is neither advertised nor loadable by exact name. An unknown name, an empty name after the prefix, a user-disabled name and a lookup failure each surface as a transcript notice **without sending anything** (L18).
- Surprising: Each completion entry is labelled with its winning source's scope (`(project)` for `project-` sources, `(user)` for everything else) in the argument-hint slot — which the menu shows but selection never inserts, so trailing instructions still follow the completed name (L18). Manual invocation always reloads the full body: the TUI does not detect a skill already in the conversation, so a repeated `/skill:` appends its instructions again, accepted because re-injection is sometimes the intent (L34).
### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-tui-steering-queue-badge.md
- Purpose: Makes the steering queue visible, so pressing Enter during a run shows that the message landed and how many are waiting.
- Key abstractions: `formatTurnStatus`; the `pendingSteering` counter; the public `agent/queued` and `steering/message` events.
- Data flow: The agent's inbox is the authoritative steering queue but is not observable from the TUI, so the badge is a live count **reconstructed from events** rather than a projection of the queue (L14). `createTuiChat` owns the counter: `+1` on each `agent/queued` for this agent whose `info.steering` is set, `-1` (floored at zero) on each `steering/message` session event as the loop drains one, and reset to zero whenever the agent leaves `running` (L17). The running status line composes a `${queued} queued · ` badge before the `Enter sends steering, Esc cancels` hint when `queued > 0` (L16).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Deriving the count from the session log alone was rejected because a cancellation clears the inbox **without logging a drain**, so the log cannot distinguish a drained message from a discarded one; the reset-on-non-running anchor is simpler and self-correcting each turn (L23). The reset lives in the `agent/status` transition, not in `setStatus`, because `setStatus` also runs on mid-turn palette changes and must not clear a live count (L19, L25). The decrement clamp is kept because loop-authored steering logs `steering/message` with no matching user-queued increment, which would drive the count negative — the zero floor keeps the badge a lower bound rather than a lie (L27).
- Edge cases / failure handling: A foreign agent's queue is ignored, and a non-steering queue is left untouched. A cancellation mid-queue clears the badge cleanly through the non-running reset, and a drain past zero is a no-op — neither can strand a stale count (L34).
- Surprising: The badge is best-effort live UI state, never persisted, so a resumed running turn starts from zero (L33). A loop continuation that keeps the agent `running` while re-enqueuing undrained late steering can transiently over-count until the next idle reset; the badge is advisory, so the window is accepted (L35).
### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-21-tui-verbose-status-line.md
- Purpose: Replaces the single static "Working" spinner with a derived phase label and two elapsed clocks, so a slow turn is distinguishable from a fast one.
- Key abstractions: the `RunningStatus` controller (loader, phase, two baselines, refresh timer); `formatTurnStatus`; the four phases `waiting` / `thinking` / `responding` / `executing`.
- Data flow: The phase is **presentation state the TUI derives from live session events**, not a session event of its own: `step/start` enters `waiting`; an `assistant/chunk` reasoning delta or reasoning block-start enters `thinking`; a text delta or text block-start enters `responding`; a `tool/call` enters `executing`; the merge-extensible event map makes every other kind fall through a default and leave the phase unchanged (L15). The label reports `<phase> <phase-elapsed> · total <step-elapsed>`, except `waiting`, which shows only the step total; the phase clock resets on a genuine phase change or a new step, the step clock on `step/start`, and tool time between `step/end` and the next `step/start` accrues to the finishing step's total (L16).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The phase is deliberately **not** a session event or an agent status — a durable, model-visible phase would demand a new session event under the model-visible ⟺ logged rule for no model benefit (L21). The controller exists only while a turn runs: a one-second `setInterval` refreshes the elapsed time, a phase event refreshes it immediately, and `clearStatus` clears the interval, stops the loader and drops the controller so no live timer survives a transition to idle or disposal (L17). A mid-turn palette rebuild carries the phase and both baselines across, so a running status never snaps back to `waiting` (L17).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Durations format as `8s` below a minute and `1m05s` at or above one. Phase detection is best-effort: an unhandled future chunk or event kind leaves the last phase in place and never throws. Exactly one `setInterval` runs per active turn, cleared on every idle or disposed transition and on shutdown (L32, L33).
- Surprising: pi-tui's vendored `Loader` could not be reused for the elapsed refresh — it animates only its spinner glyph and its dist is not the harness's to change — so the TUI owns a separate one-second interval (L23). The status line therefore reads e.g. `Thinking 4s · total 8s — Enter sends steering, Esc cancels` (L31).
