### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-22-durable-subagent-catalog-and-list-agents.md
- Purpose: A durable inventory of session-backed subagents plus the model-facing `list_agents` projection, so a parent can discover the children it may still talk to.
- Key abstractions: the `subagent/descriptor` log event as the *sole* subagent discriminator; the mode-discriminated descriptor (`one-shot` / `continuable`); `SubagentRuntime.listChildren(parentSessionId)`; the closed `SubagentListEntry` union (`kind: 'child' | 'diagnostic'`); the `list_agents` tool's `scope: 'children' | 'descendants'`.
- Data flow: Enumeration uses `ctx.sessionQuery.traceSession(parentSessionId)` to obtain the parent's direct live-preferred children, then reads and validates each candidate's descriptor **without activating the child**; candidates without a descriptor are silently omitted, and a candidate that is unavailable or corrupt produces a per-child diagnostic; results are returned in stable `createdAt` then child-id ascending order (L18–25). The `list_agents` adapter filters to continuable children, refines status through the live Agent registry (`running` / `idle` / `ready`), and renders `<id> [<status>] — <label>` (L56).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The session header gains **no `kind` discriminator** — an ordinary `ctx.sessions.fork()` also creates a direct child, so the descriptor is the only evidence that a traced child is a subagent; adding a header field would still not carry the reconstruction data, and it would create a second classification source (L35, L72). Corpus construction is an **all-or-nothing trust boundary** (a persistence listing failure or a live/persisted header conflict fails the whole call) while per-child inspection failures are isolated to one candidate, so one damaged sibling does not remove visibility into every healthy child (L33, L86). The service keeps `sessionQuery` **optional** so start and follow-up work without it, and the `list_agents` tool plugin requires both `subagents` and `sessionQuery` at load, so a deployment can use `send_message` without installing session query (L39).
- Edge cases / failure handling: A candidate with more than one descriptor is corruption rather than evidence of another Activation, and is diagnosed without another read (L35). The listing path checks the abort signal before and after each await and normalises any post-abort rejection to `SubagentError` code `CANCELLED` (L41). `activity` is a process-local corpus snapshot: `inactive` encodes neither successful completion nor resumability, and `running` says only that the session is live — a live continuable Agent outside the manager's matching Activation still appears `running` while `send_message` rejects it as an ownership conflict (L37). For already-current storage a torn child tail is surfaced rather than repaired, because `inspect()` returns the valid stored prefix while the resume path's repairing `load()` closes it (L107).
- Surprising: The note publishes its own complexity: in the persisted-only worst case the work is **O(D × C + Σ L_i)**, not merely O(D), because each exact read rescans persistence and reloads the full candidate log — the first version accepts this as the no-index correctness baseline and says deployments must treat total corpus and child-log size, not just direct-child count, as the capacity constraint (L43, L105). Remote ACP one-shot runs stay outside the catalog because they publish no local child session, and enumerating them would require a separate durable local record "rather than pretending remote lifecycle ids are session ids" (L113).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-24-provider-retry-policies.md
- Purpose: Lets a deployment give one provider an effectively unbounded retry policy while every other provider keeps a bounded one — with the policy owned by the *route* that actually failed.
- Key abstractions: an optional `retryPolicy` inside each adapter's provider configuration, resolved at registration and exposed via `providerRetryPolicy()`; the `normal` and `always` modes; `@deepseek-ai/dsh-llm-retry`; the non-surface `llm/retry` event.
- Data flow: When a call enters its final adapter boundary, `ctx.llm` binds the serving registration's immutable policy to that call, so the loop passes it to closed-step recovery even if the route is disposed or replaced while the request is in flight (L16). The listener reads the provider from the durable `request/header` in force when the failed step closed — excluding later recovery mutations — and never re-resolves policy from the mutable registry; it derives a canonical key from every field of the resolved policy, sorting `retryableCodes` because eligibility uses set membership (L38).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The effective policy is **route-owned registration state**, not a retry-executor setting: a retry-executor-level switch could not isolate the unbounded cost to one provider and could silently apply after runtime rerouting (L16, L48). A very large finite retry count was rejected because it "eventually violates the requested keep-retrying contract and serializes an arbitrary operational limit as if it were meaningful" (L52). A separate exact-provider list on `dsh-llm-retry` was rejected because it duplicates route names outside their owning adapter configuration and lets registration drift from recovery policy (L50). The provider error text and discarded partial output must **not** enter the next model request: the error is operational state, not conversation content, and including it would change the retried request instead of repeating the failed one (L44, L62).
- Edge cases / failure handling: `always` mode asks downstream recovery first so a specialised policy such as context-overflow compaction can make progress; a downstream retry wins, and a downstream failure or thrown recovery error falls back to an unbounded retry of the same provider request with the thrown error logged (L40). The retry listener owns and drains delegated recovery before cancellation or plugin disposal can finish, and success, turn cancellation and plugin disposal are the only termination paths. An over-cap provider `Retry-After` makes normal mode delegate, while always mode keeps its guarantee by using the configured local backoff (L42). `always` events omit `maxRetries` (UIs render `∞`) because JavaScript `Infinity` cannot be stored in a durable session event (L44). A positive provider `Retry-After` within the cap stays exact and unjittered.
- Surprising: Changing a route's limits, code membership or backoff starts a **new** retry count and initial delay even when the mode is unchanged, because the canonical key covers every field (L38). Omission still selects the shared core default of five retries for every composition, and a layered config may retain `maxRetries`/`retryableCodes` after switching to `always` — the resolver ignores those inactive fields while still rejecting unknown keys (L16). Provider-SDK retries were rejected because hidden attempts multiply agent-level budgets and may splice or discard streamed output without a reconstructable retry record (L60).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-24-model-facing-session-query-tools.md
- Purpose: Exposes the trusted `ctx.sessionQuery` service to the model through five narrow read-only tools, without handing over storage authority.
- Key abstractions: `@deepseek-ai/dsh-tool-session-query` registering `session_search`, `session_event_search`, `session_trace`, `session_event_trace`, `session_event_read`; the internal split into `input.ts` / `service-boundary.ts` / `workspace-access.ts` / `operations.ts` / `presentation.ts`.
- Data flow: Every executor derives its caller from the immutable `ToolExecution.exec.agent` identity and **never accepts a model-supplied workspace**; a target is authorised only when its observed `cwd` exactly equals the caller session's `cwd`, and the header returned by the same service observation is validated before any payload renders (L24). Every trusted service call crosses one model-boundary sanitizer that checks the execution signal first, records the diagnostic chain on a best-effort basis with a fixed placeholder for uninspectable values, and falls back to the fixed generic `SESSION_QUERY_TOOL_FAILED` code (L26).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Exposing provider cursors to the model was rejected because recording a tool result or starting the next model step changes the relevant generation, so a cursor is usually stale before the model can reuse it (L44). The tools expose **no** cursor, offset, page size or model-controlled result limit: one execution follows provider cursors internally while the observed generation stays valid and collects up to `maxSearchResults` (default 100), and a capped result tells the model to narrow its query (L32). The consumer does **not** implement its own truncation or spill — the generic `tools/post-execute` spill policy owns inline byte retention, so session-query tools cannot disagree with other tools about oversized results (L10, L34, L45). The search tools expose *prior work* rather than the operation performing the search: `session_search` omits the caller's own session, and `session_event_search` on the current session intersects the requested range with the event before the current `step/start` (L28).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Lineage rendering stops at an unauthorised ancestor or descendant subtree **without revealing the hidden session id** (L24). A caller whose session has no `cwd` can inspect only its own session; missing agent identity fails closed. A missing, malformed or failed title stays isolated to that id, preserves the base result, renders an unavailable marker and logs the underlying error, while an authorisation mismatch fails closed (L36). Because internal pages share generation-bound cursors, both search tools are **exclusive** in the agent-loop scheduler, while the exact trace and read tools opt into parallel sibling execution because their observations tolerate intervening commits (L32). The search execution carries a `searchTimeoutMs` (default 30 s) through the tool deadline and the service abort signal; cancellation starts no queued title inspections and rejects the complete execution after already-started inspections settle (L36).
- Surprising: The shipped TUI, Web and headless compositions deliberately do **not** mount the consumer, so their default requests carry neither the query prompt nor the five schemas; the automation-only ACP composition mounts neither (L40). Exact string `cwd` equality is chosen over resolving symlink-equivalent paths because it favours a conservative security boundary (L56).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-23-tui-status-prompt-tools.md
- Purpose: Extends `/status` to show the instructions and capabilities the next model request will actually receive.
- Key abstractions: `ctx.systemPrompt`; the same renderer the agent loop uses; the assembly's ordered tool list.
- Data flow: `/status` assembles the current agent's system prompt through `ctx.systemPrompt` using the command's cancellation signal and the current agent scope, so scoped sections, variables, tool restrictions and assembly listeners match a request made at that point; after the bordered diagnostics card, unbordered `System prompt` and `Registered tools` sections show the rendered prompt and the ordered tool names (L14, L16).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Reading prompt sections and the tool registry independently was rejected because that bypasses prompt-assembly waterfalls, tool ordering, presentation modes and per-agent restrictions, so the diagnostics could disagree with the next request (L20). Only tool *names* are shown: they answer which capabilities are registered without letting the card be dominated by parameter JSON, and full schemas remain in the generated catalog and source definitions (L22).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Prompt and tool values are escaped through the TUI's terminal-control sanitizer before rendering; empty prompt text renders as `(empty)` and an empty tool list as `(none)`. Assembly failures surface through the existing command-error notice. The snapshot is point-in-time — a later registration, restriction, mode change or dynamic provider can alter the next request (L16, L26).
- Surprising: The command can therefore run prompt providers and assembly listeners, exactly like request preparation does, and that is accepted rather than avoided (L26).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-25-subagent-policy-inheritance.md
- Purpose: Makes a delegated child start under the parent's *explicit* policy choices, so delegation cannot widen a parent that has switched to a restricted mode.
- Key abstractions: `captureDelegatedPolicyOverrides` / `appendDelegatedPolicyOverrides` in `dsh-subagent`; the captured `permission/preset` identity (auto / danger-full-access), the `sandboxPolicy.overrideOf(parent.session)` snapshot, and the pinned `'never'` approval policy; the `source: 'delegation'` marker.
- Data flow: The delegation boundary captures the overrides **before its first await**; the capture becomes a `permission/preset` event for an inherited identity and source-tagged `sandbox/mode` and `approval/policy` events during the child factory's unpublished setup (L14, L16). Because `Session.firstLiveSeq` is fixed after the constructor seed while `Session.inheritedEventCount` keeps the exact fork-prefix length, the inherited facts follow fork history without changing its lineage cut, and the existing last-event-wins folds make the delegation snapshot beat stale fork history (L16).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Only the Auto or Full access identity and the **explicit** sandbox session override are copied — never deployment defaults and never one-shot grants, because defaults remain operator-owned and may change; an unswitched parent stamps nothing, so its child follows the current deployment (L14, L33). Live resolution walking `parentSession` at each call was rejected because it breaks the "two sessions never see each other's state" isolation invariant, requires the parent to stay loaded for the child's lifetime, and makes a mid-run parent switch retroactively change a running child — snapshot-at-delegation is the semantic (L34). A first-prompt listener was rejected because the creation transaction already permits log appends before publication (L32). Generic `SessionHeader` policy fields were rejected because they duplicate an event-sourced fact in metadata (L30).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Auto review does not turn the inherited preset event into an authorisation receipt — each child call is classified again (ordinary project-local work is low risk; medium-risk work needs explicit authorisation naming action, target and scope; high-risk work is always denied), and `parentSession`, the creation prompt and `agent-message.senderSessionId` are sufficient to recover that context without a new call id, delegation record, review receipt or Session migration (L18). A confined child gets the ordinary denial marker and its escalation request is rejected deterministically by the pinned `'never'` policy, with a `subagent:delegation` runtime-context statement telling the child to report the limitation instead of retrying (L24). The keyless headless snapshot is the assembled regression: only the parent is `read-only`, the deployment default is `workspace-write`, and the child's persisted event plus the denied disk write both fail if capture is removed (L41).
- Surprising: A grandchild folds its parent's *logged* state, so the rule composes without another inheritance mechanism (L16). Each delegation adds at most three log-only events, and compositions without the optional permission-preset, sandbox-policy or approval services behave unchanged (L42).
### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-22-web-multimodal-image-input-and-durable-attachments.md
- Purpose: The durable image pipeline — a browser paste becomes an immutable stored object plus a role-neutral `ImageBlock` in the session log, so a model-visible image is reconstructable from the log alone.
- Key abstractions: `ImageAttachmentRef` and the role-neutral `ImageBlock` in the merge-extensible `ContentBlockMap`; the `AttachmentId` branded as `sha256:<digest>`; the `PromptInputPart` intake union; the `ctx.attachments` seam; the `packages/attachment/attachment` + `attachment-local` implementation pair.
- Data flow: The browser sends base64 once across a narrow intake union; the front door validates canonical base64 and MIME, then calls `AttachmentStore.saveImages()` with the whole decoded batch. The service owns count, aggregate and individual byte limits, full raster/MIME agreement, intrinsic dimensions, decoded-pixel count and master preparation, and **prepares and verifies every batch member once before publishing any member**, so one malformed image cannot create partial references (L118). The host then calls the agent with normalised text and durable image blocks in wire order. The local backend stores content-addressed objects under `$DSH_HOME/attachments/v1/objects/<prefix>/<sha256>` with owner-only permissions, synchronising every ancestor on a home's first save because "existence is not treated as durability — another process may still be between `mkdir` and parent `fsync`" (L73).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The **persistence boundary is message acceptance, not paste** — unsent files stay temporary client-owned draft state, because immediate persistence creates durable objects before a session or message owns them and needs quota, orphan-lifetime and cleanup policy (L37, L181). Inline base64 in messages and logs was rejected because it duplicates binary data across RPC, events, history pages, forks, compaction and browser storage and invites token accounting to treat encoded text as model text (L185). A specific `ImageBlock` beats one generic `AttachmentBlock` because provider semantics are modality-specific — images are native multimodal input while PDFs may be provider files or extracted text (L193). The host is the authoritative preflight point and rejects an image prompt for a model that explicitly excludes image input **before** writing an attachment or event (L126).
- Edge cases / failure handling: A failed send restores the complete text and image draft without clobbering text or images added while the request was in flight (L30). If a later storage I/O operation fails, the caller appends no model-visible event and receives no partial references, though an earlier content-addressed object may remain unreferenced (L118). `session.attachment` serves bytes only when a durable event in that session references the identifier, and the client rejects invalidated late loads *before* allocating an object URL (L120). Version one performs **no automatic deletion** — reference-aware garbage collection needs its own design because an age-only rule can delete data a durable session still references; reads verify the digest and recorded metadata without reapplying current admission limits, so lowering policy does not invalidate older history (L75).
- Surprising: A Workspace switch moves a mixed text-and-image draft only when the destination shell accepts the complete image batch; refusal leaves both parts with the source (L71). Provider-neutral token estimation does **not** guess visual pricing from image dimensions — provider-reported usage remains authoritative (L132). The note records that a missing or corrupt object makes exact model reconstruction fail, and that failing loud preserves integrity even if it prevents the session from continuing until repaired (L224).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-26-code-mode-trajectory-waterfall-spans.md
- Purpose: Adds Code Mode sub-call structure and real per-sub-call wall time to the two analytical views — trajectory and waterfall.
- Key abstractions: the snapshot's `codeDispatches` index; the `subtool` cell kind with a `Sub` tag and a 28px indent; `deriveSubSpans`; the `timing` provenance tags `measured` / `running` / `unknown`.
- Data flow: The trajectory layout fold takes the snapshot's `codeDispatches` index and, after each Tool cell whose `callId` has dispatches, interleaves one `subtool` cell per sub-dispatch in start order; a settled sub-call's duration is its start/settle pair and a running one shows the em dash, "exactly the native in-flight convention" (L18). The waterfall folds the dispatch index into per-turn lanes with **real** timing: each parent's dispatch window is first start → last settle, and every lane's offset and width is its fraction of that window, so parallel sub-calls visibly overlap (L19).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Waterfall sub-spans were deliberately deferred until a real start/settle pair existed, because "a span without real timing would have been a lie" (L12). The `unknown` timing tag covers a settle-only replay window (`callTime: null`), drawn hollow and titled "duration unknown" — **never a fabricated 0 ms** (L19). Folding sub-calls into the turn-span node counts was rejected because it hides exactly the structure the stack exists to show (L24). A separate sub-call panel was rejected because the stack's settled UX is nesting under the parent everywhere (L26).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Both views read `codeDispatches` through the standard snapshot hook — no new wire data, no new stores — so replay renders identically to live by construction (L20). A running lane extends to the window end at reduced opacity. Trajectory cell indexes now count sub-calls, so `#N` totals grow on Code Mode turns (L32).
- Surprising: The waterfall carries the **first REAL wall-time rendering in the client**, while the turn bars remain node-count stand-ins — the contrast is deliberate and labelled by hover titles (L32).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-26-ptc-dispatch-ui-foundation.md
- Purpose: Makes a PTC mode `run_code` turn render with the same fidelity as a native tool call — a labelled card, a full-content dispatch payload, and a way to enable the mode.
- Key abstractions: the required `run_code` `description` parameter (bash's exact contract); the `tool/code-dispatch` event carrying `content: ContentBlock[]` + `isError`; the temporary `DSH_TOOLS_MODE` env var (`native` | `ptc` | `both`).
- Data flow: `run_code` gains a required `description` (active voice, 5–10 words, shown in the UI; whitespace-only rejected at execute); `presentCall` now titles the card with the description and moves the program to `rawInput` (L18). `tool/code-dispatch` logs the sub-call's **complete model-facing outcome** in the `tool/result` vocabulary, replacing `resultSummary` and deleting the summarise/cwd-normalisation machinery outright, so a UI renders a sub-call through the identical code path as a native result (L19). The event stays log-only: `deriveMessages()` ignores it, so nothing about model context changes.
- Non-obvious design decisions: Keeping a bounded summary (raised cap, or a cap plus a `truncated` flag) was rejected because any cap forces a second, degraded render path plus truncation UI; the accepted cost is that a program reading a large file logs the rendered content verbatim on the dispatch event — uncapped, outside spill policy, growing the session log by the same bytes (L24). Logging the canonical `value` instead of rendered `content` was rejected because `tool/result` persists content, not values, and native parity means matching that exactly (L28). The `DSH_TOOLS_MODE` env var is explicitly a **temporary** hook: the flag grammar and a profile key would both harden a seam the per-session design intends to remove (L26).
- Edge cases / failure handling: The worker code runtime is mounted unconditionally because Loader metadata was static when this shipped — a native boot only registers the service, and workers spawn per run (L20). The released v0 edge freezes the accepted historical dispatch payloads and preserves them into v1; malformed mixtures refuse before publication. Existing ptc snapshot fixtures were re-recorded (L32).
- Surprising: The note states the model-visible surface grew: the `run_code` schema (one required parameter) and every ptc system prompt and tool-schema snapshot changed, so a UI-fidelity change to a *display* label propagates into the model's own schema (L32).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-25-workspace-ui-product-flow.md
- Purpose: The complete Workspace/Session product flow — what the Host owns, how the client models pending Workpaces and Sessions, and what happens on the first send.
- Key abstractions: the durable Workspace entity and its ordered `sessionIds` index; the frontend object layer (`SessionManager`, `WorkspaceManager`, `SessionRuntime`, `WorkspaceRuntime`); the "Intent" stage; the `conversation` slots (`startSession`, `updateSessionPrompt`, `sendSession`, `open`, `toggleSidebar`).
- Data flow: A frontend Session **preallocates a SessionId** when created and owns its Intent target and `pendingPrompt`; it remains the same object after Host `session.create` succeeds (L39). The first send advances through Workspace materialisation → Session attachment → prompt sending, in order, and each failure returns to the same Session: a failed Workspace creation leaves the Workspace Intent holding its input and error, a failed Session creation returns the Intent to an editable state and retries **with the same preallocated SessionId**, and `workspace-attach-failed` proves the Session was published so the same object enters the real list and retains the prompt (L60–65).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Eagerly creating a Host Session for the zero state was rejected — a page with no input would enter the Host lifecycle and change refresh semantics (L94). The Workspace domain uses a durable marker to distinguish "never initialised" from "initialised but empty", and bootstrap is reentrant and writes the marker last; when the marker is absent the Registry calls only `SessionPersistence.list()`, reads no history and parses no event bodies (L33). Membership requires **both** that an id appear in the Workspace's index **and** that the canonicalised `SessionHeader.cwd` equal the Workspace path; `SessionHeader` does not gain a `workspaceId` (L31, L102). Object identity, not a record swap, is the mechanism for surviving RPC and Host frames arriving in either order (L68, L90).
- Edge cases / failure handling: If the page switches to another Intent while a Session is being created, the old Session does not send automatically even if it is later published; it keeps its original prompt and visible error (L66). Two Workspace indexes claiming the same Session is corrupt state and **fails loudly** (L31). Deleting a Workspace registration removes the group but retains the directory and session logs; its Sessions become Ungrouped, and a reload reconstructs the same result from the independent baselines (L80). Historical Sessions with a missing or invalid cwd remain Ungrouped, and there is no manual-adoption entry point in this iteration (L119).
- Surprising: The default Workspace is chosen by the **maximum `updatedAt` of its member Sessions** (falling back to `createdAt` for an empty Workspace), and that derived value chooses only the default target — it does not alter the Host order or trigger another selection after later hydration (L50). The Host account remains the manual `Workspace.sessionIds` order — a newly attached Session is placed first and activity does not mutate it — while the grouped browser may instead select a browser-local recent-update view, so two ordering models coexist deliberately (L74).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-23-web-permission-and-approval.md
- Purpose: Puts the web host on the same sandboxed product path as ACP, and makes the approval question actually answerable in the browser.
- Key abstractions: the composed sandbox rows (`dsh-sandbox-local`, `dsh-sandbox-policy`, `dsh-bash-sandbox`, `dsh-fs-sandbox`, `dsh-user-approval`, `dsh-permission-presets`); `BootHostOptions.sandbox`; the `approval/request` waterfall answerer; the `session.permissions` / `session.setPermission` unary RPCs; the `PermissionOption` DTO; the `conversation.composer` chain.
- Data flow: The proxy's `approval/request` answerer reads the approval id from the session's just-appended `approval/asked` audit event (an ask with no audit event is a foreign channel and delegates), mints one stable rpcId per question, broadcasts the answerable `approval/requested` frame to every open mux stream, and **replays still-pending frames verbatim on each mux open** (L16). `respond` routes by the echoed rpcId, validates the payload with the existing zod schema, cross-checks its audit correlation against the routed entry, resolves the answerer and broadcasts `approval/resolved`; the ask's abort signal withdraws the question as `cancelled`. The permission select projects `ctx.permissionPresets` into a protocol-owned DTO, and idle switches are held last-write-wins in a proxy-side pending map and flushed on `agent/pre-step`, because knob events must stay turn-enclosed for durable replay (L18).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Reusing the ACP `session/set_config_option` shape was rejected because the web contract's unary method registry is its own dialect and a generic config-option surface would bypass the compiler-locked schema table for one select (L24). A session event for pending interactions was rejected because answerable requests are transient interaction state, not durable session data, and persisting requested frames would re-ask dead questions on replay (L26). The answerer is registered even when no mux subscriber exists, because the pending entry must survive client disconnects and a subscriber-gated answerer would fail asks closed during a reload window (L28). Optimistic card removal on click was rejected: the broadcast resolved frame is the truth, because removing on click would hide a question a rejected receipt or transport failure left standing; the panel disables its buttons locally and re-arms them on failure instead (L30).
- Edge cases / failure handling: The `hasOpenTurn` fold moved to `dsh-session` and replaced the private copies in `dsh-user-approval`, the ACP bridge and the proxy, so one definition decides turn-enclosure (L18). The sidebar mirrors every blocked interaction with an amber warning dot that outranks the running ring, including during search; tracking is per request identity rather than by reading Session instances, and it clears per connection generation so reopen replay is authoritative. Sessions never instantiated still light their dot (L20).
- Surprising: The composer's bottom-row chip hosts the `PermissionSelect`, while a pending approval **takes over the composer** entirely — the `ApprovalPanel` replaces the InputBar with a justification headline, the paired command and one-shot refuse/allow buttons (L20).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-23-web-todo-display.md
- Purpose: Gives the web client a plan surface, by consuming `todo/write` as a Session **side effect** rather than a surface node.
- Key abstractions: `applyEventSideEffects`; `rebuildDerivedFromWindow`; `ConversationSnapshot.todos`; the `conversation.input.dock` slot; the keyed `tool.call.toolview` slot.
- Data flow: `applyEventSideEffects` gains a `todo/write` case (whole list, last write wins) and clears on `turn/start` (L18). `rebuildDerivedFromWindow` sweeps the window from an empty plan and restores the tail-page seed **only** when the window never determined the plan (no `todo/write` and no `turn/start`); otherwise the in-window write/`turn/start` fold wins. The host answers a tail request with the projection or omits it when no plan stands, so **an absent field is the authoritative empty list** (L18). `TodoPanel` mounts through the `conversation.input.dock` slot and reads the host-computed `todos` projection via `useProjection` — no store, no service, no ctx; the inner component stays props-complete and framework-free and the dock adapter is a one-line wrapper (L22).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Folding todo writes into `nodes` as surface entries was rejected because a replayed window would render every superseded list, and the event is deliberately not a surface type (L30). The absent-field rule matters on rollback: a live write whose host crashed before persisting leaves the log empty, and preserving the prior value would strand the rolled-back plan on screen indefinitely (L18). Hardcoding the panel inside `ConversationRoot` was rejected because the dock slot is the architecture's home for always-on strips above the composer, and a hardcode bypasses the slot registry's disposal and ordering (L31).
- Edge cases / failure handling: A dedicated `todo_write` chat row is a plain registrant plugin whose summary derives from the call args (`N/M done · first active item`, with a `+<n>` count of the other active ones in `ToolRow`'s non-shrinking `summarySuffix` slot); unparseable args fall back to the generic row summary (L26). The panel is hidden while empty, and collapses to a header of title plus `·`-joined per-status counts with zero-count segments omitted (L22).
- Surprising: The tail history page carries `todos` — the full-log standing plan computed independently of the page window — so a reopened session restores the plan when it still stands and the last write precedes the window; that value survives an older-page prepend, is overwritten by any later write, clears on a later `turn/start`, and resets to empty when a tail response carries no projection (L37).
### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-22-docked-web-goal-bar.md
- Purpose: Gives the browser its first goal surface — a composer-context strip with phase, objective and edit/pause/clear actions.
- Key abstractions: `GoalBar` as a props-driven component registered second in the composer's input-dock list (after Todo, before Queue); `GoalBarActions` in ui-goal's slot contract; the host-computed `goal` projection.
- Data flow: The runtime session gains the goal surface through the host-computed `goal` projection: the history tail seeds its whole current value and `session/projection` frames update it when durable `agent/inbox/spliced` insertions commit goal snapshots or clear tombstones (L20). The four rendered mutation verbs fold transport failures into `{ ok: false }` results like every sibling session method; the runtime session resolves the current goal's compare-and-set ref internally, so the UI passes no ref.
- Non-obvious design decisions: Visibility drives the label and actions — active shows "Ongoing Goal" with pause/edit/clear, paused swaps pause for a resume icon, blocked carries `blockedReason.message` as the tooltip (L16). A completed goal renders nothing: "a completed goal is history, not chrome". Goal *creation* lives on the `/goal` command, not the bar, and the bar is a status indicator rather than a creation surface (L14, L32). Every mutation first acquires a synchronous component-local **single-flight latch**, because React's pending-state render cannot close the same-frame click window (L16). A successful clear suppresses that exact goal id immediately while the authoritative null projection catches up, so an acknowledged tombstone cannot leave a stale control that submits `GOAL_NOT_FOUND` (L16).
- Edge cases / failure handling: The edit form closes only when the edit succeeds; a failure preserves the draft and displays the error in the bar, and resume/clear failures are displayed there too. Clear calls `onClear` directly with no confirmation because a clear keeps a durable tombstone, so nothing is unrecoverable. An effect keyed on the goal's id resets the transient state and drops the edit form when the goal's identity changes, so neither a cleared marker nor a surviving draft can affect the replacement goal (L16). A `Loading goal…` placeholder was rejected because the strip would flash and collapse on every session open (L31).
- Surprising: The strip's background is a translucent hover alias rather than a literal colour, because the translucent gray "resolves to that value over the white light-theme base and lifts the strip off the composer card in dark mode, where a static light token would sink" (L22).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-22-web-bind-address.md
- Purpose: States the web host's network exposure policy explicitly rather than letting the carrier pick.
- Key abstractions: `WebServer`'s required `host: '127.0.0.1' | '0.0.0.0'`; the `dsh web` CLI's loopback-only choice.
- Data flow: The CLI binds `127.0.0.1` and **rejects** `--host 0.0.0.0`; the carrier passes the required host to `node:http` with no fallback of its own (L16, L18).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The carrier is left generic so a custom composition's policy stays visible at its package interface, while the product CLI owns the stricter loopback choice (L18). A boolean exposure flag was rejected because `--host 0.0.0.0` names the resulting socket behaviour directly and matches the underlying server option without introducing a second term (L24). Defaulting inside `startWebServer()` was rejected because the carrier has multiple possible shells and no basis for choosing their deployment policy; requiring `host` makes the choice visible at every assembly call (L28).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Keeping an explicit all-interface mode was rejected because authentication alone does not supply TLS, forwarding semantics, or a supported remote-deployment contract for a tool-capable Host (L26). The process-token and browser-cookie authentication does not broaden the deployment contract.
- Surprising: The note separates *carrier capability* from *product policy* — the carrier still accepts `0.0.0.0`, so a custom composition can still expose it and must own every consequence (L32).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-23-session-telemetry-otel-revival.md
- Purpose: Revives a session-telemetry seam with a **mandatory redaction point**, plus an OTel reference backend — after legal review rejected the earlier branch's verbatim export.
- Key abstractions: `SessionTelemetrySink` (`emit`/`flush?`/`shutdown`), the service-registered `SessionTelemetryBackend`, `SessionTelemetryCoordinator`; the `session-telemetry/record` waterfall; `@deepseek-ai/dsh-session-telemetry-otel` with `DISABLED` / `FEEDBACK_ONLY` modes.
- Data flow: Each new Session object starts capture immediately before `firstLiveSeq`; the per-append firehose deep-copies, redacts and hands off every event with zero I/O; re-adopting the same object resumes after its module-scope cursor. Ledger identity is `(session.id, session.format_version, event.seq)` so receivers can deduplicate (L16).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The seam ships **no redaction rules of its own** — the innermost `next()` is a pass-through and deployments mount their rules as listeners; a throwing rule withholds the record fail-closed, and redaction applies to the exported copy only so the canonical log is never rewritten (L17). A built-in conservative rule set was rejected because "as an SDK we cannot know which patterns are secrets in a given deployment, a shipped list invites false confidence ('redaction is on') while catching only known shapes" (L30). Receiver-side redaction was rejected because it ships the secret first and scrubs it second (L28). The boundary axiom is that the harness's aspect ends at `emit()`; batching, retry, queueing and loss policy belong to the reporting SDK (L22).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Delivery is best-effort and a crash can lose queued records; backend retry or losing the module-scope cursor can duplicate lifecycle-local rows, so receivers deduplicate on the ledger identity (L22). `DISABLED` is the plugin default and constructs no transport; removing the entry is a silent opt-out. Replaying constructor seeds is opt-in via `includeHistory` and never happens on construction, restoration or HMR (L34).
- Surprising: The note records that forwarding the seam's turn-boundary `flush()` hint to the provider's `forceFlush()` **shipped and was then removed**, because three distinct silent-loss paths shared the wrapper state — a dispose racing an in-flight flush, overlapping hints displacing the retained promise, and the provider's fixed 30-second flush timeout rejecting while the processor still drains (L36). Reinstatement is allowed only by calling the retained `BatchLogRecordProcessor`'s own `forceFlush()`, never the provider's timeout-wrapped one.

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-23-trajectory-step-cell.md
- Purpose: The trajectory tab's reusable step row and turn-list chrome, with own-duration times and token columns.
- Key abstractions: `TrajectoryCell` (38px step row, kinds User / Message / Tool); `TrajectoryTurn`; `deriveTrajectoryLayout`; `ConversationNode.time`; `ToolResultNode.callTime`; `RunningToolCall.time`.
- Data flow: `deriveTrajectoryLayout` expands assistant `blocks[]` into cells, pairs tool-calls with `tool-result` by `callId` into a single Tool row, folds `partial` and `runningCalls` (deduped), hangs usage on Message only, and builds group descriptions as a wall span plus a tool histogram (`1.5s bash×6`) (L18). Duration rules: User `+0s`; Message = assistant.time − previous surface time (including skipped context); Tool = result.time − callTime when both are known; in-flight Tool = `—` (L20).
- Non-obvious design decisions: There is deliberately **no Think row** — a single `assistant/message.time` cannot yield a Think own-duration without chunk-level clocks, so the row is omitted rather than showing `—` (L24). Separate Call and Result rows were rejected because Result had no own duration to show; one Tool row carries the call→result interval (L26). Cumulative elapsed from session/turn start was rejected — the Time column is each row's own duration. `user/message` has no wire turn, so each User row is enclosed in the next assistant/steering turn, else the in-flight `partial` turn, else `lastAssistantTurn + 1` (L18).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Context nodes emit no cell but still advance the Message duration cursor, so a skipped context block is still counted in the elapsed time. In-flight tool durations via `Date.now()` were deferred; in-flight Time stays `—` (L32). The selected state draws a 2px inset ring and is not wired to chat selection.
- Surprising: The layout is built from a pure derivation over the snapshot, and the cell trailing columns share the Turn header geometry (`320 = 4×71 + 3×12`) so the two columns line up exactly (L17).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-23-tui-file-reference-autocomplete.md
- Purpose: Gives the TUI a dependable way to discover workspace paths while composing a prompt, without paying for the file's contents.
- Key abstractions: a bounded, cancellable host-workspace path index rooted at the session's working directory; the `@` token grammar; the config for result count, index size and excluded directory basenames.
- Data flow: Typing `@` at a token boundary fuzzy-matches files and directories; queries containing `/` list the named directory directly; paths with whitespace use the `@"path with spaces"` form. Selecting a file changes **only the editor text** — the submitted user message retains the natural `@path` spelling and carries no injected contents, hidden context or reference object (L16). When the model-facing `read` tool is registered, the TUI contributes a stable system-prompt section identifying `@` paths as explicit user references, directing the model to call `read` when contents are needed, and forbidding claiming inspection before that call. Tool results invalidate the reusable fuzzy index so later interactions observe likely workspace mutations.
- Non-obvious design decisions: Eagerly injecting selected file contents was rejected: it spends tokens before relevance is known, can capture stale content before execution reaches the reference, and bypasses the auditable `read` call/result sequence (L22). Using the filesystem service's ordinary directory-list operation was rejected because that seam is optimised for exact model-facing operations, may represent a remote namespace, and recursive fuzzy indexing would multiply provider round trips and couple editor latency to tool policy (L26). Requiring an external file finder (`fd`, `rg --files`) was rejected because it would make baseline completion vary by host installation (L24). A new cross-package file-search capability was rejected because the TUI is the only consumer and the behaviour is editor presentation, not a model capability (L28).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Completion is deliberately bounded and advisory — large workspaces may omit paths beyond the index cap, ignored files may still appear (traversal does not follow directory symlinks or interpret ignore files), and remote deployments must align the TUI host working directory with the `read` namespace or supply a different completion surface (L14, L32). The default exclusions are `.git` and `node_modules`.
- Surprising: Structured session mentions keep their existing snapshot preparation, because "unlike files, a referenced session has no general model-facing retrieval tool, so reducing `@session` to a path-like label would make its content unreachable" (L18).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-23-web-assistant-markdown.md
- Purpose: Renders assistant Markdown safely and consistently in the web conversation, in both the streaming tail and settled history.
- Key abstractions: `MarkdownText` in `@deepseek-ai/dsh-client-ui-primitives`; `mdast-util-from-markdown` plus the GFM micromark extensions; the shared `CodeBlock` and the client's shiki singleton; `mathCompatibility`.
- Data flow: `ui-conversation` selects `MarkdownText` only for assistant `text` blocks; user and steering messages render through `projectUserText` and stay literal. The parser works incrementally while a turn streams, and fenced code highlights incrementally by tokenizing newly completed text from a saved grammar state plus the still-growing last line, excluding the completed prefix from repeated work (L16).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Parsing happens at the **presentation leaf**, not into session snapshots, because durable React nodes or presentation ASTs would reintroduce a final-versus-streaming mode boundary and make presentation state part of the runtime projection (L34). Raw HTML stays inert source text because **no HTML parser enters the pipeline** — raw HTML has no product need and enabling it would enlarge the executable-content boundary, so a sanitizer dependency was declined (L24, L36). Link destinations are restricted to absolute HTTP, HTTPS and mailto; relative destinations and other protocols render as non-navigable text, and HTTP(S) links open in a new tab with `rel="noopener noreferrer"` (L24). A micromark attention extension lets runs of at least two asterisks close after Unicode punctuation when followed immediately by CJK text — a narrow tokenizer-level exception that keeps the upstream resolver rather than rewriting source (L16, L40).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Streaming stays literal for maths until finalization so incomplete formulae do not flash errors. The inline-code URL rule fires only when a token consists *entirely* of an absolute HTTP(S) URL, so commands, partial URLs, other schemes and fenced code stay inert (L18, L42). Fenced code and GFM tables own horizontal overflow so long content cannot widen the conversation column.
- Surprising: The note records that the original decision to promote the existing mdast/micromark dependencies and maintain a custom React walker was **later reversed on new evidence** — incremental streaming parsing needs AST-level input a string-only wrapper cannot provide (L30).
### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-24-configurable-tui-prompt-theme.md
- Purpose: Makes the terminal prompt row composable — deployments can choose field order, replace the input prefix, add plugin state, or build a Powerline prompt.
- Key abstractions: the TUI theme groups `color`, `truecolor`, `leftPrompt`, `rightPrompt`, `inputPrompt` and the static `inputPlaceholder`; `ctx.tuiPrompt`, a context-global registry supplied by `@deepseek-ai/dsh-tui/prompt`; `register(name, initialValue)` returning a handle with `set(value)` and `dispose()`.
- Data flow: The three prompt strings interpolate `${name}` references; unknown or unavailable values disappear with adjacent horizontal separator whitespace. The renderer reads current values with `get(name)` and subscribes with `subscribe(listener)` to learn when to redraw (L16). Values are stored **strings, not callbacks**: updates are explicit, unchanged strings are ignored, and a registration, mutation or disposal schedules one coalesced notification (L16).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Rendering-time plugin callbacks were rejected because they add an avoidable failure boundary to the render pass; stored strings keep it free of plugin evaluation (L24). The change notification is a **direct in-service callback, not a Cordis event**, because the notification has exactly one consumer — a global typed event would add a bus entry, scoped-dispatch surface and cross-plugin fan-out nobody observes (L26). Semantic style roles were rejected because they cannot express arbitrary Powerline background transitions without expanding the shared style protocol for each presentation technique (L28).
- Edge cases / failure handling: The coalesced notification contains **every** observer — a synchronous throw, a rejected returned promise, and even an error hostile to string rendering (logs go through the non-throwing `errorChain`) — so one broken observer cannot starve the rest, and delivery re-checks each subscription's liveness so a listener that synchronously unsubscribes another in the same burst silences it immediately (L16). Each `subscribe` is a distinct subscription keyed by record identity, so two fibers may pass the same callback and disposing one leaves the other live. Registration follows Cordis effect ownership, rejects duplicate names and removes the value on plugin disposal. Registered fragments are **trusted ANSI-capable presentation output** — a prompt-value plugin may emit terminal controls (L18).
- Surprising: The built-in `cwd`, `git/worktree`, `token_meter/cache_hit_rate`, `model`, `context`, `queued`, styled `symbol` and `indicator` caret values use the same registry, so the steering-queue badge and the animated caret are just two more registered values (L20). Changing `inputPrompt` through a registered value preserves editor text, cursor, history, completion and focus because pi-tui supports replacing equal-width first and continuation prefixes in place (L36).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-24-new-session-clears-to-empty-state.md
- Purpose: Makes the sidebar "New Session" land on the designed empty-state launch page instead of a blank transcript.
- Key abstractions: `SessionsService.clear()`; the `conversation.empty` branch; the shared `InputBar` with `variant="hero"`.
- Data flow: `SessionsService.clear()` wipes the persisted selection and `list.current`; the top-level creation entries (`onCreate()` with no cwd — New Session and New Workspace) call `clear()` so `AppFrame` renders `conversation.empty` (L14). The empty state's first send still runs `conversation.startSession` (create → open → send) and reuses the same `InputBar` component as the resident composer. Per-project "+" (`onCreate(cwd)`) keeps create-then-open until the empty-state picker can accept a seeded cwd.
- Non-obvious design decisions: A second empty chrome inside `ConversationRoot` was rejected because it duplicates the launch InputBar and breaks the "empty→content ruling that one InputBar moves position rather than swapping components" (L18). A dedicated route or slot was rejected for this pass because `conversation.empty` already owns the launch UI and clearing `current` is the existing empty branch (L20).
- Edge cases / failure handling: New Session no longer mints a host session until the first send, and reloading after clear stays on the empty state. The workspace chip's "Create new" path runs `sessions.createWorkspace` → host `session.create` (mkdir recursive) → `sessions.open`, so a default session lands in the new workspace. `InputBar` paints the bottom chrome with local native `<select>` state only — the host plan, access and model seams remain unwired (L24).
- Surprising: The hero uses a soft ellipse glow width-locked to the card using the figma asset ratio (`1051/776`) so the glow scales with the card rather than with the viewport (L24).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-24-tui-question-dialog-multiline.md
- Purpose: Makes a pending question readable inside configured width and height bounds, with paging when the content does not fit.
- Key abstractions: `InlineModalComponent`; `renderOptionBlock`; `questionDialogWidth` / `questionDialogMaxHeight`; `maxQuestionOptions`; the shared FIFO with model and plugin overlays.
- Data flow: The question renders as an inline modal **between the transcript/status area and the editor**, because placing it at the terminal edge separates the pending decision from both the transcript that prompted it and the input that follows it (L14). The effective height is additionally clamped to the current viewport after reserving the editor, so the editor stays below the question during resize (L16). The option-line budget subtracts padding, header, position and footer rows before `windowBlocks` runs; the window obeys both the count cap and the remaining row budget, keeps the selected option visible, and renders omitted options as `↑ N more` / `↓ N more` markers (L19).
- Non-obvious design decisions: When the question text or `detail` exceeds the header allocation, the header becomes a **paged line viewport** with its own `… lines A-B/N • PgUp/PgDn` status row, and Page Up/Page Down traverse both line viewports — forward exhausts the header/detail pages before entering oversized selected-option pages, and backward reverses the order — so plan-review detail stays reachable instead of being stranded behind the height clamp (L18). Ellipsis-only truncation was rejected because it signals lost text without making the description readable; wrapping the combined label and description was rejected because a composite row couples their widths and either side can starve the other (L26, L28). Pushing the bounds into pi-tui was rejected because generic overlay slicing cannot identify option boundaries, selected content, controls or the inline editor relationship (L32).
- Edge cases / failure handling: If fixed chrome would leave fewer than four option rows, the compact header becomes the line pager so selected content, paging status and both option markers still fit. The explicit `↑ N lines hidden` fallback is reserved for a viewport below the configured minimum, where the whole semantic layout cannot fit (L17). The model-facing schema, selected labels, abort/cancel behaviour and ACP elicitation path are unchanged (L42).
- Surprising: Descriptions consume additional rows, so fewer options can be visible than `maxQuestionOptions` — the count cap and the row budget are enforced together because the count cap cannot contain wrapped blocks by itself (L34, L38).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-24-tui-shell-prompt-editor.md
- Purpose: Replaces the framed pi-tui editor with a two-line shell-style prompt.
- Key abstractions: a DSH-owned context line of independently prioritised segments; the fixed-width `dsh> ` prefix with an equal-width continuation indent; a pnpm patch to `@earendil-works/pi-tui` adding `frame: "none"` and fixed-width prompt prefixes to `EditorOptions`.
- Data flow: The context line shows the working directory, running-turn timing, optional Git branch, current model, token totals, cache hit rate and context pressure as independently prioritised segments; narrow terminals omit lower-priority segments while retaining the directory, then running timing when present (L14). The second line's running steer/cancel guidance is placeholder text that disappears when input begins. Input, explicit newlines, autocomplete, cursor placement and scroll indicators share the reduced first-row width, and automatically wrapped rows render no prefix so their text starts at the editor's left padding and wraps at the full content width (L16).
- Non-obvious design decisions: The patch stays limited to the published editor JavaScript and declarations, and the exact dependency pin is kept so installation either applies the known patch or **fails** rather than silently dropping the presentation (L18). Filtering the rendered editor output in a wrapper was rejected because it would depend on recognising ANSI-styled border and scroll-indicator rows and distinguishing autocomplete output from input output — undocumented render details (L22). Vendoring the complete pi-tui package was rejected because the project updates frequently and the change needs only a localised editor rendering option (L24).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Prefixes must have equal visible widths; construction fails when they differ. A pi-tui upgrade requires reviewing and reapplying or retiring the patch. The default remains the upstream horizontal frame, so only the DSH editor opts in (L16, L34).
- Surprising: The internal segment representation establishes width priorities **without exposing a public customization language**, and the note frames future Starship-like configuration as something that can build on it only after the default modules and overflow behaviour have production evidence (L32).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-24-web-session-model-selector.md
- Purpose: Gives the web composer a visible, mutable session model selection sourced from the Host, with one atomic switch boundary.
- Key abstractions: `ModelSelection` installed per Agent; the `session.models` directory RPC and `session.selectModel`; the `conversation.input.model` session-scoped single slot; `ModelDirectoryResolver`; `resolveCallConfig`.
- Data flow: The provider/model/reasoning selection comes from the latest `request/header` when the session has used a model, otherwise from `ctx.agentDefaultModel`. `session.selectModel` assigns the session-local selection and **prompt assembly captures it with request routing**, so a switch during a running step applies to the next assembled step; the next consumed selection persists through the full `request/header` snapshot, while a choice that has not reached a request stays process-local (L14).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Catalog membership stays **advisory**: `session.models.current` is returned independently and can remain routable when absent from every group, and the Host does not synthesise an unlisted row after its provider stops advertising it. The two surfaces answer that state differently — the TUI renders the unlisted current model as its own row, while Web shows the unset trigger label and asks for a replacement, because Web is the surface where a catalog is edited (L16). Persisting every click as a new session event was rejected because a choice is not model-visible until prompt assembly consumes it, and the existing `request/header` already records the first request that uses the route (L36). Rejecting changes while an Agent is running was rejected because the atomic selection snapshot separates the assembled step from the next selection, so the user can prepare the following step without altering the in-flight request (L34).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Provider catalogs and exact metadata load concurrently by provider and **fail independently**, so successful groups remain usable alongside retryable failure records. Directory and selection calls share an operation generation so an older response cannot replace a newer result; a connection reset discards the process-local projection before restoring the Host selection, and failures retain the previous selection and usable groups (L18). Selection uses `resolveCallConfig` to reject unsupported effort ids and materialise an adapter-configured default. A model without reasoning metadata has no Effort row, and the provider-default row appears only when the adapter does not configure a model default.
- Surprising: The production browser roster is assembled from two config files, and the model feature is one `dsh.client` row rather than a package hardcoded in Web boot code, with Cordis service injection waiting for the conversation slot before registering the composer occupant (L22).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-25-session-list-browsing-and-manual-order.md
- Purpose: Fills in the session-list interactions — a flat view, hover cards, action menus, workspace renaming and manual ordering inside a group.
- Key abstractions: the group-by modes `WorkSpace` / `In one list`; `WorkspaceView.sessionIds` as the manual order; `workspace.rename` and `workspace.insertSessionBefore`; the two-fact shell/region contract `{ wide, expandSidebar }`.
- Data flow: The `session/event` → `touchSession` activity-pinning chain is **deleted wholesale**; new sessions prepend at attach and explicit reordering goes through `workspace.insertSessionBefore({ workspaceId, sessionId, beforeSessionId? })` with DOM insertBefore semantics (L32). The UI is HTML5 drag on session rows inside a group; the drop only sends the RPC, the client performs zero local reordering, and the view refreshes from the response upsert and the changed frame — a failed move changes nothing (L34). The client's upsert rejects snapshots older than the installed projection so a late unary response cannot roll back a newer frame.
- Non-obvious design decisions: Two ordering authorities cannot coexist: keeping activity pinning and treating drag as a transient adjustment "would make it a fiction; two coexisting ordering authorities cannot be explained to the user", and a middle ground (freeze pinning after the first drag) adds a state tier with murkier semantics (L42). A numeric `index` in the reorder payload was rejected because it drifts during the drag window — after the host prepends a new session the same index points at a different row — while anchor-style insertBefore is naturally immune to prepends and filtered projections (L44). Optimistic client-side reordering was rejected because it needs failure rollback and another entangled state, and local/LAN round-trips are millisecond-scale (L46). Nesting sessions by fork lineage was rejected because nesting makes the current child visible only while its ancestors are expanded and limits in-group ordering to root nodes; `parentId` is lineage data, not a list-navigation structure (L50).
- Edge cases / failure handling: `workspace.rename` trims the title and requires a non-blank value; the same-title no-op and the duplicate check both evaluate inside the Host's **serialised workspace-operation chain** shared with path adoption and deletion, so concurrent workspace operations cannot interleave a duplicate or an out-of-order fake success, and a conflict returns `workspace-name-conflict` (L28). The entity throws a typed `WorkspaceMoveInvalidError` only for unaccounted session/anchor ids; the handler maps exactly that to `workspace-move-invalid` while storage failures stay internal. The detail card and the row menu are mutually exclusive — no card while a menu is open or a drag is in flight (L22).
- Surprising: The browsing region moved wholesale from ui-sidebar into ui-workspace so that every future workspace-domain dialog (the Delete confirmation is next) lands in one package, and ui-sidebar shrinks to column geometry (L48). Flat mode supports neither reordering nor a create-in-workspace entry point — an accepted scope reduction (L56).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-26-ptc-chat-subcall-rows.md
- Purpose: Makes a PTC mode `run_code` turn render its sub-calls *identically* to native tool calls, without lying about the transcript.
- Key abstractions: the runtime's `ToolCallTree` folding in-window `tool/code-dispatch-start` / `tool/code-dispatch` events into a private per-parent index; recursive `ToolCallBlock.subCalls`; the whole-Tool `'conversation.chat.tool'` seat and the keyed `'tool.call.toolview'` slot.
- Data flow: The fold projects running and settled children onto recursive `subCalls`; live Session projection and `projectConversationHistory` share it, and copy-on-write parent arrays plus path-copy projection keep unrelated roots and siblings reference-stable (L18). Every atomic call dispatches through the same keyed slot with `entryKey = Tool name` and the same `GenericToolCard` fallback, so a keyed registration takes over descendant and top-level calls without registration changes. Running parents receive accumulated dispatches in the same recursive block, so child rows stream in during the run (L19).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Sub-calls are standard Tool call blocks attached recursively **outside the surface flow** — they never join `nodes`, because "the model made one call" and nesting preserves the code↔calls association while leaving the fold's model-visible-order invariant untouched (L18, L25). Hiding them until the parent row expands was rejected by product decision: "the sub-calls ARE the story of a PTC mode turn" (L27). A dedicated sub-call row component was rejected because the whole point is identity with native rows; the nest wrapper (indent + left edge) is the only sub-call-specific chrome (L29). The events are narrowed structurally at the wire-consumer boundary, which also rejects cyclic parent relationships (L18).
- Edge cases / failure handling: A new `code` row variant (classifier `run_code → code`, `Code` title) summarises with the model-authored `description` and expands to the program itself rather than the args JSON envelope. `materialFor` recursively searches `nodes` and `runningCalls`, so a selected descendant callId resolves to full args and complete output through the identical path as a native settled call. Selection highlighting reaches nested rows through the same `selectedCallId` channel (L21, L33).
- Surprising: Custom toolview registrations apply to sub-calls **for free, and deliberately** — there is no per-registration opt-out short of a component reading its own context, which no current consumer needs (L33).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-26-ptc-dispatch-log-spill.md
- Purpose: Limits the durable copy of PTC sub-dispatch results with the existing spill implementation, undoing the unlimited-log cost the host foundation accepted.
- Key abstractions: the `tools/ptc-dispatch-log` scope-filtered waterfall on the registry; the registry's private `shapeDispatchLog` invoker passed as a capability closure in `RunCodeBridgeOptions`; `dsh-spill-policy`'s listener for this event.
- Data flow: The bridge runs the waterfall over each settled sub-dispatch before appending `tool/code-dispatch`; the payload carries the outer execution, the `agent` routing key, the sub-call identity and the default content (the rendered result projection a native `tool/result` would carry, while the program receives the structured `value`). A listener can replace **only the durable copy, which the model never sees** (L18). The spill artifact is labelled `dispatch` under the sub-call id, and UIs and replay read its full text through the same path used for spilled native results, so both result kinds render with the same information (L19).
- Non-obvious design decisions: A plain byte limit inside the bridge without spill storage was rejected because truncation without a locator loses data replay or UIs may need and restores the less informative "truncated summary" rendering that earlier changes removed (L24). Spilling inside the bridge by calling `ctx.spillStore` was rejected because the registry would then require the spill capability; the waterfall keeps the policy with the other spill decisions and lets a composition omit it (L26). Reusing `tools/post-execute` for nested calls was rejected because post-execute can change the program-facing result, so nested calls deliberately skip it and programs receive complete data (L28).
- Edge cases / failure handling: If a listener throws, the invoker reports the thrown value safely and **uses the original settled content** (L18). When more than `maxParallelSubCalls` log tasks are pending, the ordered commit loop waits, so a slow spill backend limits later sub-call starts instead of accumulating unlimited pending I/O; run settlement still waits for every task inside the open turn. One deliberate difference: the model-result listener skips `read` to prevent a `read → spill → read again` loop, while the dispatch-log listener also replaces oversized `read` sub-call content because a log copy is not model context (L20).
- Surprising: Old logs with oversized dispatch content still replay, because the event fields are unchanged — only future appends contain less text (L32).
### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-06-29-todo-write-tool.zh.md
- Purpose: Chinese translation of `2026-06-29-todo-write-tool.md` — a line-for-line parallel rendering of the `todo_write` decision (same 61 lines, same schema table and the same four test tiers).
- Key abstractions: Same `todo_write(todos: [{content, status}])` tool, same `todo/write` `SessionEventMap` variant, same `pending | in_progress | completed` triple.
- Data flow: Same whole-list replace on the event-sourced log, and the same re-derivation from the latest `todo/write` with no later `turn/start` (L22).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Preserves the "not a surface event" rule (it is durable UI state travelling alongside the conversation, not part of it) and the minimum-item-shape argument — each dropped field is one less thing the model must produce on every call (L26, L30).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-06-14-acp-multi-session.i18n.yaml
- Purpose: Bilingual-pair consistency record for the ACP multi-session multiplexing note — the git blob hashes of its English and Chinese sides as of the last confirmed-consistent state.
- Key abstractions: two `path: sha1` lines under a four-line header that names the repair command `pnpm run verify-translation-pairing --write .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-06-14-acp-multi-session.md`.
- Data flow: The translation-pairing verifier compares the two recorded hashes against the working tree, so a one-sided edit to either `.md` becomes a mechanical CI failure rather than a review-time accident.
- Non-obvious design decisions: The header states that both languages carry equal authority, so the Chinese side is a maintained twin edited in lockstep rather than an artefact regenerated from the English.
- Edge cases / failure handling: A mismatched hash pair is the only failure mode; the file is machine-written and carries no prose of its own.
- Surprising: A 50-line decision note about per-session demultiplexing is pinned by 6 lines of YAML, so the *pairing* is stronger than the content — which is exactly the point of the record.

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-24-configurable-tui-prompt-theme.zh.md
- Purpose: Chinese translation of `2026-07-24-configurable-tui-prompt-theme.md` — a line-for-line parallel rendering of the TUI prompt-theme decision (same 40 lines, same four rejected alternatives).
- Key abstractions: Same `ctx.tuiPrompt` context-global registry with `register(name, initialValue)` / `set` / `dispose`; same `get` / `subscribe` surface; same theme grouping of `color`, `truecolor`, `leftPrompt`, `rightPrompt`, `inputPrompt` and the static `inputPlaceholder`.
- Data flow: Same registry semantics — stored *strings* rather than callbacks, unchanged strings ignored, one coalesced notification per registration/mutation/disposal, and the renderer repaints through the in-service `subscribe` callback rather than a Cordis event (L16).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Preserves the two rejections that matter most for transfer: render-time plugin evaluation (a failure boundary on the render path) and publishing the change as a global typed event with no second consumer (L24, L26). It also keeps the trust decision that a prompt-value plugin may emit arbitrary ANSI (L18).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Same containment rules — a synchronous throw, a rejected promise, or an error hostile to string rendering cannot starve the other observers, and liveness is re-checked during delivery so a listener that unsubscribes another in the same burst silences it immediately (L16). Duplicate registration names are rejected and disposal removes the value (L16).
- Surprising: Faithfully keeps the fact that each `subscribe` is a *distinct* subscription keyed by record identity, so two fibers may pass the same callback and disposing one leaves the other live (L16) — a subtle ownership rule that is easy to lose in translation.

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-24-model-facing-session-query-tools.zh.md
- Purpose: Chinese translation of `2026-07-24-model-facing-session-query-tools.md` — a line-for-line parallel rendering of the model-facing session-query decision (same 56 lines, same five tools and five rejected alternatives).

Spill stays with the generic `tools/post-execute` policy, and the consumer imports no `ctx.spillStore` (L34).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Preserves the workspace-authority rule that a target is authorized only when its observed `cwd` *exactly* equals the caller's session `cwd`, and that the check is re-validated against the header returned by the same service observation before rendering (L24). It also keeps the cursor rejection and the oracle-resistance argument for pre-authorising parent ids (L44, L46).

- Surprising: Keeps the honest note that a Web composer showing the unset label can still send with its current routable selection, and that a custom composition which mounts the tool without spill *explicitly accepts* complete inline traces and reads (L16, L56).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-24-provider-retry-policies.zh.md
- Purpose: Chinese translation of `2026-07-24-provider-retry-policies.md` — a line-for-line parallel rendering of the per-provider retry decision (same 72 lines, same eight rejected alternatives and the same YAML example).
- Key abstractions: Same per-adapter `retryPolicy` inside provider configuration, resolved and exposed through `providerRetryPolicy()`; same `mode: normal | always` with `maxRetries`, `retryableCodes` and a `backoff` block (`initialDelayMs` / `maxDelayMs` / `jitterRatio`); same `@deepseek-ai/dsh-llm-retry` listener.
- Data flow: Same capture point — when a call enters its final adapter boundary `ctx.llm` binds the serving registration's immutable policy to that call, so the agent loop still hands it to closed-step recovery even if the route is disposed or replaced mid-flight (L16). The listener then reads the provider from the durable `request/header` and derives a canonical key from every field of the resolved policy, so replacing a route with different limits or backoff starts a new count and initial delay (L38).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Preserves the ordering rule that `always` mode asks downstream recovery *first* so a specialised policy such as context-overflow compaction can make progress, and that a downstream failure falls back to unbounded retry of the same request (L40). It also keeps the jitter formula — the sample is applied *before* the cap — and the rule that an over-cap provider `Retry-After` delegates in normal mode but falls back to local backoff in always mode (L42).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Same draining rule: the retry listener owns and drains delegated recovery before cancellation or plugin disposal can finish, and only success, turn cancellation and plugin disposal are termination paths (L40). The `always` event omits `maxRetries` so no JavaScript `Infinity` is ever written to the durable session log (L44).
- Surprising: Faithfully keeps the "put the error into model context" rejection — a transport or provider diagnostic is operational state, not conversation content, it can expose sensitive provider details, and it would change the retried request instead of repeating the failed one (L62). It also keeps the honest statement that an always policy can spend unbounded requests on *permanent* authentication, quota, invalid-request, protocol or context failures (L70).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-24-tui-question-dialog-multiline.zh.md

It also keeps the reason the bounds are not pushed into pi-tui: generic overlay slicing cannot identify option boundaries, selected content, controls or the inline-editor relationship (L26, L32).

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-24-tui-shell-prompt-editor.zh.md

### .agents/notes/archived/feature/2026-07-24-web-session-model-selector.zh.md
- Purpose: Chinese translation of `2026-07-24-web-session-model-selector.md` — a line-for-line parallel rendering of the session model-selection decision (same 44 lines, same six rejected alternatives).
- Key abstractions: Same `ModelSelection` installed by the Web Host for every created or resumed Agent; same `session.models` directory and `session.selectModel` RPC pair; same `ModelDirectoryResolver` with `idle`/`loading`/`ready`/`selecting`/`error` states; same `conversation.input.model` session-scoped slot.
- Data flow: Same sourcing rule — the provider/model/reasoning selection comes from the latest `request/header` when the session has used a model, otherwise from `ctx.agentDefaultModel`; prompt assembly captures the selection together with request routing, so a switch during a running step applies to the *next* assembled step (L14).
- Non-obvious design decisions: Preserves the two "advisory vs authoritative" splits: catalog membership is advisory and `session.models.current` is returned independently, so an unlisted current model stays routable, while the Host does not synthesise an unlisted row (L16); and the two front ends answer the unlisted case differently — TUI renders it as its own row, Web shows the unset trigger label and asks for a replacement, because Web is where the catalog is edited (L16). It also keeps the rejection of hardcoding the DeepSeek catalog in the browser (L28).
- Edge cases / failure handling: Same generation guard — the directory and selection calls share an operation generation so an older response cannot replace a newer result, and a connection reset discards the process-local projection before restoring the Host selection; a failure retains the previous selection and the usable groups (L18). Unsupported effort ids are rejected by `resolveCallConfig`, which also materialises an adapter-configured default (L16).
- Surprising: Faithfully keeps the "persist every click as a new session event" rejection — a choice is not model-visible until prompt assembly consumes it, and persisting unused UI intent would add a durable event that cannot reconstruct a model request (L36).
